Changelog

Unreleased

0.11.1 - 2026-11-27

  • The page of a mechanism walk is now a lesson player (G430). kube-noob learn mechanism <id> -f DIR --format html drew a grid of boxes with "Previous hop / Play / Next hop" under a banner, which showed the verdicts and taught nothing about where the things on the path stand. It is now a port of KubeLearn's player (MIT, credited on the page and in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md): your own objects as cards on an isometric cluster floor (ING shop, SVC orders-api, DEP orders-api), a marker that flies from one hop's object to the next and leaves the card in its verdict, a caption under the scene, a story column with the hop's whole explanation and its object, rules, verdict and findings, the list of hops, a sequence view, an Events drawer of the evidence, dark and light themes, and an overview that states the path verdict, the exit code and what it means. What the old page guaranteed is unchanged: one self-contained file with no URL in it, the same bytes for the same input, nothing from a manifest as markup, the walk's exit code. What a rule did not judge is still never drawn as passing: a hop no manifest can show is a dashed actor marked "not in any manifest", the hops after a stop are grey and "not walked", and green is used only for a hop that holds. Without JavaScript the page is the whole walk as text. While the walk is on a hop the card it stands on shows that hop's verdict (an earlier break on the same card stays beside it as hop 1 and a cross), a policy the walk only counts is named on its card when a finding names it, and the camera never crops an actor or puts one under the caption. A hop's explanation was capped at 600 characters on this page; it is now whole (G411's rule, one view further).
  • A mechanism lesson names its worked examples (G423). Each walkable lesson carries examples:, the fixture directories (relative to tests/fixtures) that show it, so a page built from the curriculum finds a new lesson's bundles instead of listing them by hand. A value that is not a plain relative path -- .., a leading /, an address -- is refused by the walk, and a walk never prints these paths, because the installed package does not have them.
  • A sixth mechanism lesson: Deploy an app (G422). kube-noob learn mechanism mech-cp-deploy-an-app -f ./manifests follows a Deployment from the manifest to a pod that is ready: nothing refused for its identity, API version, selector or limits; requests the scheduler can fit; every ConfigMap, Secret, claim and ServiceAccount a pod names present; images by digest; a readiness probe. The four hops a running cluster decides (admission and storage, the controllers, the scheduler, the kubelet) are marked not checked. A pod that names a Secret the bundle has no kind for stops the walk rather than passing. Measuring first also found that containers: [] is silent everywhere in the audit; it is recorded, not fixed here.
  • A fifth mechanism lesson: a packet meets a NetworkPolicy (G421). kube-noob learn mechanism mech-net-network-policy -f ./manifests follows what decides whether a packet to one of your pods is admitted or dropped -- the namespace has a policy, each policy selects a pod that is here, no ingress rule is open to every source, the selected pods keep a route to DNS -- and marks the two hops no manifest records (which CNI enforces the policy, and the per-packet decision) as not checked. Measuring the rules first found three things nothing reads, and the hops say so rather than hold on them: a workload no policy selects, a rule with ports and no from, and a policy port no container declares.
  • A fourth mechanism lesson: a Pod calls a Service (G420). kube-noob learn mechanism mech-net-pod-calls-a-service -f ./manifests follows a call by name -- the namespace's policy, egress to cluster DNS, the DNS answer, the Service and its ports, the workload, readiness and the ClusterIP translation -- and marks the two hops no manifest records as not checked. Each rule was measured with its object absent before the hop was written.
  • A sixth release video: the walk you can watch. demos/video/stage/videos.json gains walk, rendered by the same pipeline as the other four animated videos -- same brand music bed, same Kokoro narration, same captions, same loudness spec, same 4K master -- and the release matrix renders it with them. None of the others shows kube-noob learn mechanism, and a walk is the one answer here that is easier to watch than to read: mech-net-request-to-pod on three inputs, drawn hop by hop. tests/fixtures/mechanism/complete holds on every hop a manifest can answer for and exits 0, with the hop across the node marked not checked and not counted as passed; tests/fixtures/mechanism/broken is BROKEN on the first hop with the rule that says so, still reaches the end, and exits 1; and the first bundle passed as one file -- a fragment of a namespace -- cannot be judged at the policy hop, so the walk stops there and exits 5, and the hops after it are drawn hollow and marked not walked, never as holding. Nothing on screen is typed: demos/video/facts.py gains a walk group that runs the command itself on each input and takes every hop, the mark the command printed for it, its reason, the finding, the verdict line and the exit code the process returned from that run, and the stage draws only what it is handed. The group refuses to render rather than mislead: it raises if the first input stops holding, if the second has no BROKEN hop, or if the third no longer stops, because each scene's caption would then sit over a different story. A new scene kind, hops, draws the path; the rail lights only as far as the last hop that was judged, so the stretch to a hop nothing checked stays dark. Two things the fifth video had hidden, found by rendering this one: a video whose only desk scene is its call to action stopped on a desk that was never built, and voiceover.py assumed every video that opens on the alarm also has a dust scene.

0.11.0 - 2026-11-26

  • The admission webhook could answer with more warning lines than it documents (G419). Ten findings plus "not fully audited" made twelve lines against a documented eleven, and the in-cluster load run failed on it. The statements now take their room from the findings, and are never dropped.
  • New rule KN-V024: an Ingress that routes to a Service that is not there, or to a port it does not serve (G405). No rule read the link between an Ingress and its backend Service, so learn mechanism mech-net-request-to-pod walked "A request finds your Pod" complete at exit 0 for an Ingress naming a Service ghost that did not exist, and for one naming port 9999 of a Service that serves 8080 -- the request gets a 503. The port is judged whenever the Service is in the input. A missing Service is judged only where a whole namespace was passed and that namespace defines at least one Service; on a fragment, or where the namespace commits no Service at all, the rule says it had nothing to judge rather than reporting a finding or reading as clean. The network lesson has a new hop for it between the edge and the Service, so it now has six observable hops.
  • A mechanism walk called a hop BROKEN on a fragment, for an object the fragment could not show was missing (G408). One Deployment at one replica pasted into learn_mechanism (or passed as one file) came back broken: KN-C007's "no autoscaler holds this at two" fires on a fragment, where the autoscaler may simply not have been pasted, and so do KN-V011 and KN-N012 about a pod that may be in another file. partial_bundle's own description promised cannot judge there. It is true now: on a fragment such a hop lists the finding and says cannot judge, exit 5; passed as a whole namespace the same hop is BROKEN as before. audit is unchanged. A lesson hop that names no kind to read is also refused now, and no hop holds over an input with no object in it.
  • A replica count that is not a number read as "two or more replicas" (G407). With replicas: "1", or a Helm expression left in a quoted string, KN-C007 said nothing and the node-dark walk printed "every Deployment, StatefulSet and ReplicaSet here declares two or more replicas" at exit 0; KN-C008, KN-C009 and KN-C010 read the same field as one replica. All four now decline on that object and name it, so audit is undetermined there rather than scored, and the walk says cannot judge. An absent replicas is still the API's default of one and is judged as before.
  • A mechanism walk answered complete at exit 0 over input whose audit exits 1 about the same objects (G406). Three shapes: a Deployment with no pod template beside a healthy one (two hops listed it among the objects they held on), a duplicate selector key on the Service the Service hop held on, and a single-replica Argo Rollout the audit names as not audited while the node-dark walk printed "no replicated workload here is left at a single replica". A hop read only the rules it lists. Now a hop does not hold on an object the validation stage calls malformed, a hop about workloads does not hold beside a pod spec nothing read, and a walk is never complete where the audit of the same input is undetermined -- each is cannot judge or stopped at exit 5, with the object named. A walk can still be complete where audit exits 1 on a finding that is not about the path.
  • A mechanism walk held the Service hop on a Service that selects nothing, and held every hop on objects from another namespace (G404). learn mechanism mech-net-request-to-pod printed "the Service selects at least one pod in this bundle" at exit 0 for a Service with no selector at all, for one whose namespace holds no pod, and for an Ingress and Service in shop beside a Deployment and NetworkPolicy in other. KN-V011 and KN-V012 walked past such a Service without recording anything, so their silence read as a verdict; they now say they had nothing to judge (notEvaluated, with the reason -- the score is unchanged, because nothing went unread), and the hop says cannot judge. A walk also judges exactly one namespace now, names it (namespaces in JSON, namespace judged: in the text view), and stops at exit 5 when the objects the path reads are in more than one.
  • A mechanism walk that found a broken hop and then stopped reported only the stop (G400). On the node-dark lesson, a replica-spread hop BROKEN by KN-C010 followed by a disruption-budget hop nothing could judge came out stopped at exit 5, and its reason named only the stop. A broken hop is a verdict about the path, so the walk is broken at exit 1 now, with stoppedAt and the hops not walked still reported and the reason naming both. A walk that stops before anything is broken is unchanged at 5.
  • KN-C007 no longer accepts an autoscaler whose minReplicas is below two as redundancy. An HPA at 1..5, or one that omits minReplicas and takes the API default of 1, lets a workload idle at a single replica, and it is now reported (G397).
  • The release could not have finished, and now can (G414–G418). Found by a red-team pass over the release path before 0.11.0: build.sh did not know the scan video the release renders; the version bump could not go green because the offline Homebrew test demanded a formula PyPI cannot serve until after the upload (that check now asks PyPI itself); bump_version.py removed the ## Unreleased heading release-ready needs and left the website figures behind; the release's ffmpeg install had no bound of its own; and a merge to main cancelled the CI run whose transcript the release renders from.
  • release-ready could not open its weekly issue, because its label did not exist (G413). Its first run took every reading and then exited 1 on could not add label: 'release-ready' not found. It creates the label first now, as risk-review does, and only after every reading is in.
  • The Dependabot ignore that was meant to keep CodeQL v4.39.4 out matched nothing (G412). It named github/codeql-action, but the workflows use the sub-actions /init and /analyze, so #140 proposed the version anyway. It is github/codeql-action* now, and a test requires every github-actions ignore entry to match an action some workflow uses.
  • optional: true on a PersistentVolumeClaim volume hid a dangling claim (G409). KN-C017 honoured optional: true on every volume reference it reads, and a claim has no such field: persistentVolumeClaim: {claimName: ghost, optional: true} was recorded as evaluated and clean while the Pod it describes stays Pending. The escape hatch now applies only where the Kubernetes API defines it -- ConfigMap and Secret volumes, projected sources, envFrom and env key references -- and a claim, or a volume plugin's secretRef, carrying the key fires as if it were not there.
  • KN-C017 fired HIGH on correct manifests once the namespace defined any object of the kind (G410). kube-root-ca.crt, OpenShift's builder, deployer and openshift-service-ca.crt, a Secret that a cert-manager Certificate, a SealedSecret, an ExternalSecret or an OpenShift serving-cert annotation in the same directory creates, a ConfigMap or Secret a kustomization.yaml there generates, and a StatefulSet's data-db-0 mounted by a second workload were each reported as "nothing in this bundle defines it ... the Pod will not run" at exit 1. Names the platform puts in every namespace and names an object in the input creates are no longer dangling (the OpenShift ones on an OpenShift target only); where a producer is present and what it makes cannot be read -- a Certificate with no secretName, a generator whose namespace is not the Pod's, a serving-cert annotation on a non-OpenShift target -- that one name is not judged and notEvaluated or partlyEvaluatedRules says why, rather than a finding or a pass. The rule also now reads the Secret a csi, cephfs, rbd, iscsi or flexVolume volume mounts with, and reports an env key reference whose ConfigMap or Secret is in the bundle and does not carry that key. Limits it does not claim past are listed in the ledger row.
  • A mechanism hop's explanation was cut at 200 characters, and the cut fell on its caveat (G411). The text view bounded every value at 200 characters with no mark, so the clause where a hop states what its rule does not read was dropped on six of the RBAC lesson's hops and one node-dark hop. Explanations are wrapped onto indented lines now, never cut; only an input value over 4000 characters is cut, and it says so.
  • A third mechanism walk: RBAC and ServiceAccounts (G401). kube-noob learn mechanism mech-sec-rbac-and-serviceaccounts -f <dir> walks from the token a pod holds to the grants behind its identity: no pod spec leaves the automatic token mount on (KN-S014), no binding names everyone (KN-S028) or cluster-admin (KN-S016), no Role or ClusterRole uses a wildcard (KN-S015) or carries a KN-S017 escalation grant, no Secret is a long-lived ServiceAccount token (KN-S027), and the API server's own authorization is printed as not checked. The two earlier lessons each shipped a hop that claimed more than its rule decided, so every rule here was measured first on an input WITHOUT its object: five abstain, so a bundle with no binding, no Role or no Secret stops the walk at that hop at exit 5 and never passes it; KN-S014 instead fires on a pod whose ServiceAccount was never passed, so its hop needs a ServiceAccount in the input and cannot be judged without one, where it would otherwise have reported one pasted Deployment BROKEN. The titles say "here" on purpose: no rule joins a binding to the role it names, so a role that is bound and not in the input is named as not read, and the walk never says what a ServiceAccount can do.
  • A mechanism walk you can watch (G402). kube-noob learn mechanism <id> -f DIR --format html writes the walk as one self-contained page: the lesson's hops drawn in order as a schematic labelled with your own objects, an event that travels hop to hop under a caption such as 02/06 Ingress/shop/shop -> Service/shop/orders-api, and a panel that opens the selected hop -- verdict, rules, objects, findings -- with play/pause, previous/next, arrow keys, space and #step-N deep links. It is a third view of the same walk the text and JSON views print, so it can reach no other answer and the command's exit code is still the walk's (0, 1 or 5). What the walk did not judge is not animated as working: the event fails at a BROKEN hop, stops short of a hop that could not be judged, and is not drawn at all for a hop no manifest can show or a hop after a stop -- both of which are labelled in words, "not observable" and "not walked". The page fetches nothing (no URL in it, and a Content-Security-Policy that would refuse one), is byte-identical for the same input, and treats every name from a manifest as text. --format html on any other command is refused with exit 2.
  • release-ready reached Ready over a reading it never took (G403). It checked that each GitHub answer arrived, never its shape, and runs without -e: an empty or Not Found check-runs answer, a changelog heading spelled [Unreleased] or saved with CRLF, a failed release lookup, all-skipped checks or a failed issue lookup each reached an answer, most of them Ready or a closed issue. Every reading is now checked for shape, and each that fails is not known, exit 1, with nothing written.
  • A release that never finished shipping would have been reported as in step (G399). release-ready compared the changelog's unreleased section with the latest tag and never read the tag's own release run, so with nothing unreleased it closed its issue as "the website is in step" -- over v0.10.0, whose release run concluded failure with four video renders, the video attach and the Homebrew push red. 4 of the last 8 release runs concluded failure and nothing said so. It now reads that run and names each job that did not succeed, beside Ready and Not ready, and as a fourth answer, Not finished, where it used to close; a run it cannot read, cannot find or that is still going is not known, exit 1, and writes nothing. The tests execute the workflow's own script against a stub gh.
  • A mechanism walk in which nothing was checked answered complete at exit 0 (G395). A hop marked observable: false never stops the walk and never counts as judged, and nothing required that one hop be judged before the path could be called complete -- so a lesson whose every hop is unobservable walked a full green page at exit 0 under "every observable hop on this path holds", which is vacuously true of a path with no observable hops. It was reachable on purpose, not by accident: no rule in this build reads the kubelet, the container runtime, cgroups, kube-proxy, iptables, conntrack, veth or the overlay, so a Node-layer lesson could be marked unobservable hop by hop to avoid the honest stop. A walk that judged no hop is now stopped at exit 5, and no mechanism lesson can be shipped without an observable hop whose rules this build really has. Alongside it, a hop's title asserted a path fact its rules never establish: hop 4 of mech-net-request-to-pod said "Network policy lets the packet in" over a deny-all policy, where the three rules behind it fire on an absent, over-permissive or inert policy and none can establish admission. Every hop of both lessons was audited against the rules' code; four over-claiming titles and three holds sentences were narrowed to what the rules decide, including two that described one unspread replica as many and spread.
  • A Pod that named five objects none of which were in the input audited at exit 0, and nothing said so (G396). A Pod naming a ConfigMap volume, a Secret volume, a PersistentVolumeClaim, a ServiceAccount and an envFrom.configMapRef, none of them present, reported one unrelated LOW finding, in a single file and in a directory alike: no rule asked the question. KN-C017 (HIGH, wiring) now asks it, for exactly those four namespace-scoped kinds, and only where it is evidence: a (namespace, kind) is judged only when the bundle defines at least one object of that kind there. The first version judged every name a directory did not define and fired on 98 correct repositories in the suite, because a correct GitOps repository commits no Secrets -- an operator or a person creates them -- and often no ServiceAccounts or claims; a gate that fails every good repository gets turned off. So a namespace that defines some Secrets and references one it does not (db-cred beside db-creds) fires and the message names what IS defined; a namespace that defines none is not judged, and notEvaluated says which kinds and why; a single file abstains as before. optional: true, the default ServiceAccount, a StatefulSet's volumeClaimTemplates and objects the rules refused never fire. Cluster-scoped referents and imagePullSecrets are out of scope by design. Beside it, a rule that fired on part of its input and had nothing to judge in the rest was given the note "a field in this input has a type it cannot read"; it gets its own reason now.
  • A release-note search that never ran was reported as finding nothing (G390). The OpenShift release notes are searched only when the text says it is OpenShift; without that, match returns no matches and a note saying it did not look, and every caller kept the list and dropped the note. Measured through two tools of the same server on identical input: diagnose returned knownIssues: [] where known_issues returns OCPBUGS-73876 -- and the CLI's own advice line pointed at a list of reported issues it then did not print. Three builders and two text views now carry it under knownIssuesNote, the key mustgather/report.py has used all along. Beside it, the fedramp_ksi description said two indicators carry a warning where four do; that number is counted from the dataset now instead of typed.
  • Three defects in the mechanism walk, found by red-teaming it (G389). It never read notEvaluated, so a rule that reached no verdict looked like a rule that found nothing: a Deployment with no pod template walked complete at exit 0 while audit on the same input exits 1, with each hop citing as proof the rules the ledger lists as unevaluated. Its text renderer printed findings[].resource raw, so a name holding newlines wrote two lines of its own at column zero, the second being the walk's own pass sentence. And the node-dark lesson's budget hop assumed KN-C009 judges a missing PodDisruptionBudget the way KN-N001 judges a missing NetworkPolicy; measured, it abstains. All three are fixed and mutation-proved.
  • A hop no rule reads was reported as a hop that works (G387). The mechanism walk fell through to holds for an observable step naming no rules, printing "no rule reads this hop" as the thing that proved it, and the path came out complete at exit 0 -- this product's one rule broken inside the feature built to show it. It did not bite on the shipped lesson, every observable step of which names a rule; it was found by writing the next one, where for most of the control-plane path the rules do not exist. Such a hop is cannot-judge now and stops the walk, kept distinct from observable: false, which is a declared absence of evidence and must not stop it.
  • A second mechanism lesson: mech-cp-a-node-goes-dark. The three hops of surviving a lost node that manifests really answer for -- more than one replica (KN-C007), allowed to land apart (KN-C010), a disruption budget that lets one go (KN-C009) -- and a fourth, the scheduler placing the replacement, declared unobservable rather than claimed. A budget under a group no rule reads stops the walk at that hop; a namespace with no budget at all is judged, because KN-C009 is the rule for exactly that absence.
  • No release has carried a video since v0.9.2, because a trailing comma failed the size check. The videos workflow asserts the 4K master's dimensions with test "$(ffprobe ... -of csv=p=0 ...)" = "3840,2160". That format emits a trailing separator on some ffprobe builds and not others -- 3840,2160, on the runner, 3840,2160 on ffprobe 6.1.1 -- so the equality holds on one machine and fails on the other. On v0.10.0 it failed every render: four 4K masters at the right size, the right loudness (-16.04 LUFS) and the right duration, all rejected by a comma, which failed attach and skipped site-videos, so that release carries no video and the site published no Videos page. The check compares the two numbers now, read one per line with -of default=nw=1:nk=1, so no separator spelling can decide it; it still rejects a master that is not 3840x2160. The header comment also said three animated videos, which has been four since the scan video merged. The first replacement for the test was still a spelling test -- it asserted "3840" in render and "2160" in render, and both numbers appear in the comment that commit added to the step, so deleting the comparison left it green. The check is executed now against a stub ffprobe that answers in either output format, so deleting the comparison and reverting to the separator-sensitive one both fail.
  • Every exit code that decides a gate was documented and admitted, never driven (G386). G384 named this as its own open item: the table check runs each command once and asserts its code is one the row admits, and all 52 rows admit 0, so a build returning 0 everywhere passed all of them. rhacs compare is what it cost -- its row said always 0 against a command that returns 1 on an unexplained policy, and its fixture has none. Each documented code now has an input chosen to produce that code, asserted by equality: nineteen nonzero codes across fourteen commands. Two findings fell out of the measuring. posture scan --fail-above cannot fire on examples/insecure-deployment.yaml at all, because that input ranks zero attack paths -- a test driving the 1 from it would have passed while proving nothing. And learn submit 0 has no shipped input by design: examples/exercise-answer.yaml is deliberately a first attempt, so it drives the 1 and the passing answer lives in the test. ticket 0 is exempt with its reason (it needs a local Jira copy, which only corpus jira --online fetches), and an exemption for a code no row documents any more now fails.
  • A mechanisms track: lessons that walk one path and stop where they cannot judge it. Every other track is organised by product and teaches what to write and what passes. This one is organised by layer and teaches what happens, in order -- and because kube-noob already distinguishes "the rules read this and it is fine" from "nothing read this", the walk grades itself three-valued against the reader's own manifests, offline. kube-noob learn mechanism mech-net-request-to-pod -f ./manifests walks the request-to-Pod path: the edge admits it, the Service finds pods, the workload owns them, policy lets the packet in, the pod reports ready. A hop holds (0), is broken with its rule id (1), or cannot be judged -- and then the walk STOPS, the hops after it are counted as not walked rather than drawn, and the verdict is that none was reached (5). Measured: a projectcalico.org/v3 NetworkPolicy mid-path stops the walk at that hop with 2 hops not walked, where the same policy under a group the rules read lets the whole path hold. A hop no manifest can evidence, such as the veth pair and the overlay, is marked observable: false, printed as not checked, and never counted as holding. The walker adds no judgement of its own: it reads the report analyze() already produced, so a mechanism walk cannot disagree with the audit it is a view of. The object a hop could not be judged on is named from the input's own metadata, so it is quoted: the terminal boundary already stops a newline forging a line, and the quotes stop a name like web\nnot audited: 0 object(s); the verdict is a pass reading as kube-noob's own prose once that newline is collapsed (G373's shape, one surface further out).
  • A fifth release video: the zero that is not a pass. demos/video/stage/videos.json gains scan, rendered by the same pipeline as the other three animated videos -- same brand music bed, same Kokoro narration, same captions, same loudness spec, same 4K master -- and the release matrix renders it with them. It is built on the adversarial corpus case 09-a-rollout-with-a-privileged-container: a privileged container inside a kind no rule reads, where every severity count is honestly zero and the honest answer is still not a pass. Nothing on screen is typed: the counts, the rule tallies, the replica count, the object's name, the reason it was not read and the exit code are all computed at render time by demos/video/facts.py through the same audit tool Claude calls, with the exit code from receipt.audit_exit_code, and the terminal frames are recordings of the real gate run. Two facts refuse to render rather than mislead: the group raises if that input ever audits in full, and again if it ever produces a finding, because either would make the video about nothing. Beside it, voiceover.py took its video list from a hardcoded choices, so adding a video left a renderer that could not render it; it reads videos.json, the list the workflow matrix and the tests already share.
  • The path the instructions tell an agent to read has not existed since G281 (G385). G281 renamed the control summary's list from notEvaluatedRules to rulesNotEvaluated and kept notEvaluatedRules as the key on each control row. Measured on examples/insecure-deployment.yaml: controlSummary carries rulesNotEvaluated with 49 entries and no notEvaluatedRules; 30 of 31 control rows carry notEvaluatedRules. The audit_manifests description, both kube-master-controller skills and two sentences of docs/use-cases.md pointed at controlSummary.notEvaluatedRules, and the generated openapi.json and skill/ reference copied it, so one string put the wrong path on six surfaces. An agent following it reads a missing key as empty and reports 49 rules that reached no verdict as nothing unevaluated. The only check on that text asserted the stale name was present, so it passed because the path was wrong; it resolves every controlSummary.<field> against a real report now. controlSummary.partlyEvaluatedRules was correct and is unchanged. No product behaviour changes.
  • helm-audit exited 0 over its own undetermined verdict, and ten table rows documented exit codes the commands do not return (G384). Measured on G370's own objects, rendered by a stub helm outside the tree: a hardened Pod beside an rbac.k8s.io/v1 ClusterRoleBinding granting cluster-admin to system:authenticated made helm-audit print verdict: undetermined and exit 0, while audit on the same two objects exits 1 -- so a pipeline running kube-noob helm-audit ./chart went green on that chart, and it is the command the published helm-charts skill hands users. The branch derived its code from max_severity() alone; validate, 130 lines above, already carried the guard with a comment naming this class. It returns receipt.audit_exit_code now, the one place the arithmetic lives. A clean chart still exits 0, a chart with a high finding 1, and helm absent 4. Beside it, rhacs compare returns 1 when a policy is unexplained and two skills called it always 0; the four audit rows and four validate rows promised 1 only for a finding, while both exit 1 on an undetermined verdict with no finding at any severity. All ten rows are corrected. The exit-contract check no longer guesses which digits in a column are codes -- the tables mark them -- and what that check still cannot see is written down in the G384 guidance row rather than implied to be covered.
  • The exit code of every command whose exit code matters was documented and never checked (G383). tests/test_directory_plugin.py is the only check that runs a documented command and compares its real exit code against the table row, and it asserted the code only for rows beginning always 0. That is 25 rows whose code cannot be wrong, and none of the 14 -- audit, validate, protocol, policy reach, posture scan, must-gather analyze among them -- whose code decides a CI gate. Rewording fix's row for G382 moved it out of that spelling and so removed its only check, in the same commit that fixed it. The column is parsed now, a column naming no code fails the row instead of passing it, and all 14 honour their contracts on their fixtures. The first version of that parse carried the same defect it was built to stop: it scanned for a bare digit, so 0; times out after 5 seconds admitted 5 and always 0, even for 3 replicas admitted 3, widening the set the command may return. A code is matched by its position now, and the mutation the scan would have let through -- always 0, even for 1 replica on audit -- fails. Beside it, _rows() feeds a parametrize, and an empty parametrize is one skipped test at exit 0, so renaming the ## Which command heading would have deleted every exit-contract check and gone green; that floor is in _rows() now and a renamed heading fails collection. No product behaviour changes.
  • fix exited 0 beside its own critical finding, and called refused input a finding (G382). fix is the only command that rewrites a manifest, so its exit code is what stands between it and kubectl apply. Both branches ended in an unconditional return 0. Measured: over a privileged hostPID Deployment it left privileged: true in the bytes it wrote, printed after.maxSeverity: critical and score: 23.0, and exited 0 -- while audit on that same written file exits 1. On a second input it printed after.verdict: undetermined and exited 0, with high and critical both 0 so only the verdict word could catch it. The exit now comes from receipt.audit_undetermined plus the high and critical counts, the same predicate validate uses, derived once so it no longer depends on --format or on -o. FixRefused moved from 1 to 4: 1 means findings, and a file the API server refuses has none because nothing read it. A hardened manifest still exits 0, and the rewritten document is still written when the exit is 1.
  • The release-verification instructions need a network, and did not say so (G378). SECURITY.md gave gh attestation verify --repo and keyless cosign verify with no mention that the first asks GitHub's API and the second needs Sigstore's trust root -- in a project whose CI proves it installs and runs with no interface and no route, for operators behind an air gap. Measured on v0.10.0, the release publishes no attestation or signature bundle at all, so the offline forms of both tools have nothing to point at. The section now separates the connected case from the air-gapped one, says what to carry across, and states that until the bundles ship as release assets an air-gapped operator cannot verify a release from the release alone. No behaviour changes; publishing the bundles is owed and is tracked in the G378 row.
  • A waiver bought a cleaner answer than not having the object at all (G379). A hardened three-replica Deployment with no NetworkPolicy exited 1 with score 94.0 and KN-N001 open. The same Deployment beside an allow-all projectcalico.org/v3 NetworkPolicy nothing could read exited 1 with the score withheld, which is right. The same two files plus a notAudited exception accepting that policy exited 0 with score 98.0 and no findings -- four points better than the input with no policy in it, and a pass at --fail-on medium the cleaner input fails. The accept path that re-opens a withheld score was written when a decline only ever meant "the rule found nothing there because it did not look"; G368 and G369 made a decline able to stand in for a finding the rule was about to make, and accepting the object then restored the score while the suppressed finding stayed suppressed. Accepting an object says the reader may stop asking to read it; it does not say what the rule would have found in it. A decline that replaced a finding is now never covered by an acceptance -- the declined row carries suppressedFinding and the report stays undetermined. This is per decline, not per rule and not per report: a waiver over a decline that suppressed nothing, such as a refused dra.k8s.io/v1 ResourceClaim no rule reasons from the absence of, still restores the score exactly as before, which is the only way a Calico, Istio or Knative shop reaches a verdict at all. A report written before this key existed reads as "no", which is what every decline meant then.
  • The conformance matrix could not see posture diff, the renderer G366's lie happened in (G374). test_every_renderer_has_an_adapter enumerated four modules and the MCP tools over manifests; drift was in neither that list nor the exemptions, so the delta had no adapter and no stated reason -- and G366's 15 findings reported fixed were found by reading the code rather than by the suite written to find exactly that. drift is a delta over two snapshots, so what "the same fixture" means for it is decided and written down: the later snapshot is the fixture as the matrix holds it and the earlier one is the state it was in when the rules still read the object it now withholds, which is the only pairing in which the report has anything to lose. One of the matrix's two predicates does not translate as written -- a report is undetermined from its notAudited rows whether or not the join exists -- so it is translated to the row: a finding listed as fixed, or an object as removed, about an object the same report says nothing read. With G366's join reverted, 38 findings across five fixtures claim a repair about five such objects, KN-S001 critical among them in three, and the matrix goes red on ten cells; with it, none do. Found and fixed by putting the adapter there: the trend behind posture report and the HTML page read its complete flag off the resources a cluster refused alone, so a day whose report was undetermined because an object went unread was drawn as a complete read -- open: 10 yesterday, open: 0 and complete: true today. It reads the report's own verdict now; a report written before that key existed is still "not known" rather than "complete". Nothing else in the product's output changes. mcp.posture_reach's exemption text described a different tool's question and now describes its own. What this measured and did not fix is in the G374 guidance row: controlStanding reports 88 clean and 0 withheld on a report carrying 10 undetermined findings, the same 88 the fully-audited input gives.
  • A KEV catalog that was read and holds nothing said it was never given (G381). Kev.describe() asked if not self.entries and answered no KEV catalog given for both an absent catalog and a present, valid, empty one -- so a reader concluded that passing --kev would tell them more, when they already had and it is empty. The three states are now distinct, and the branch was redundant besides: vuln/assess.py already prints its own sentence when no catalog is loaded, so describe() only ever runs on a catalog that was given. In the same read, parse walked past every entry that is not a mapping or carries no well-formed CVE id without counting it and never looked at the count the catalog declares for itself: a catalog declaring 1200 entries of which one parsed described itself as 1 CVEs. Skipped rows are now counted and a shortfall against the declared count is named. A declared count is input, so it is believed only as a non-negative integer within bounds. A well-formed catalog's description and every CVE lookup are unchanged.
  • policy reach printed a forged line of its own output from a policy's name (G373). The notes and reasons name the policy that decided, and that name came out of metadata.name raw. The terminal boundary keeps the newline on purpose, because it is cleaning multi-line text, so a ClusterNetworkPolicy named payments\nnot audited: 0 object(s); the verdict is a pass\nnote: every ClusterNetworkPolicy was evaluated made --format text print three lines where kube-noob wrote one, two of them the document's own and one of them carrying the word pass; the same name reached undeterminedBy and the Undetermined: summary, and in JSON it arrived as one string with two embedded newlines. The same was open on a NetworkPolicy's and an AdminNetworkPolicy's name and on a label value in a peer selector, which every denial's reason echoes. A name or a value the API server would not accept is now made printable, collapsed to one line, cut and wrapped in <> or quotes -- so the note still says which object and which field is wrong, which is the half of it the reader needs, and it can no longer pose as kube-noob's own finding. Field values already went through repr and were not forgeable; they were unbounded (a 4005-character tier printed in full) and printed a mapping or a float nan as Python source, and they are bounded now. Beside it: example.com/v1alpha2 ClusterNetworkPolicy, a vendor CRD sharing only the kind name, was read with the upstream v1alpha2 semantics and answered DENIED with a fix addressed to a cluster administrator, because the apiVersion was matched by its suffix; and a subject mapping with an integer key beside a string key ended the whole command with a TypeError. A well-formed policy's verdict, tier and reason are byte-identical to before.
  • The Helm fixture planted its fake binary where the product refuses to run one (G376). test_helm_audit_prints_one_json_document writes its own fake helm and needs no real Helm, but it put it inside the working directory -- which helm_binary excludes on purpose, because a binary from the tree under review must not be run by the tool reviewing it (G280). On any machine without a system Helm the command returned exit 4 with empty output and the test failed, which read as "Helm is not installed" rather than as the fixture asking for the one thing the product refuses; in CI a system Helm answered first, so the fake was never exercised and the test passed for the wrong reason. The fake now sits beside the working directory instead of inside it, so it answers on every machine. No product behaviour changes: the guard is unchanged and was right.
  • validate exited 0 on a report that said undetermined, and posture scan --fail-above exited 0 beside every object it could not read (G370). On a hardened Pod beside an rbac.k8s.io/v1 ClusterRoleBinding, audit exits 1 and validate exited 0 -- having printed verdict: undetermined in the report above it, because it derived its exit code from the severity counts instead of the verdict. It now asks the same one predicate audit asks. Separately, posture scan --fail-above did not fail on an object the input held and the scan could not read: a ClusterRoleBinding granting cluster-admin to system:authenticated under that unserved apiVersion left it at exit 0 with paths: [] and refused: [], and so did an Argo Rollout carrying a privileged container. Such an object is now reported under refused, as the command's help already promised, and the gate exits 5 -- the scan withholds a pass for exactly the objects audit does not audit, which it already refused on the same inputs. A readable input still exits 0 from both commands, and --fail-above absent still does nothing on its own. If your bundle carries a kind kube-noob has no reader for, posture scan --fail-above now reports incomplete where it reported 0; a notAudited exception accepts it, as it already did for audit.
  • The plugin submitted to the Claude plugin directory was the one a community organization may not publish (G380). The directory accepts a command hook, an MCP server that runs on the user's computer, and files that are not Markdown, text, JSON, YAML or an allowed image only from Verified organizations. plugin/kube-noob is made of exactly those: a PreToolUse hook, a stdio MCP server started with uv run, 118 .py files, uv.lock, pyproject.toml, an .svg and three .tmpl files. Each refusal named a category rather than a defect, so no edit to that folder could clear them. plugin/kube-noob-skills -- the same seven skills driving the CLI you install yourself, eleven files, no hook, no server, no code -- clears all of it, and docs/operations.md now says to submit that folder and why. Both plugins stay: plugin/kube-noob is what ships once the organization is Verified and what the root marketplace.json still offers first to anyone adding this repository as a marketplace directly. Fenced example commands in the published skills use NAME, NS and POD instead of angle-bracket placeholders, so no command extracted from them can exceed the directory's limit of three < characters.
  • The claim that an absence never reads as a pass is now sixteen inputs anyone can run, with the honest answer recorded beside each one (G377). corpus/ holds sixteen case directories of Kubernetes and OpenShift manifests built to make a scanner under-report -- a metadata.name carrying real newlines and the word "pass", a Calico NetworkPolicy beside a readable Deployment, cluster-admin bound to system:authenticated under a group one segment short of the real one, a privileged container in the 201st template of an Argo Workflow, a pod spec under spec.template.Spec, an object that stops being readable between two snapshots, a duplicate items key on the List wrapping a refused object, and more -- each with a honest-answer.yaml recording what the object is, the verdict word and exit code, what the answer must name, and one sentence on the mechanism by which a tool that does not model its own incompleteness reports less than is there. There is deliberately no comparison with any other tool and no scoreboard: a league table invites an argument about the table, a reproducible corpus does not, and the README says so. python scripts/corpus_run.py drives the real CLI over every case, reads each command's own verdict word out of that command's own output, runs each case twice to compare the bytes, and fails on a disagreement rather than adjusting the record -- so the corpus is also a regression suite, and it runs in CI with the rest of the suite. Nothing in the product changes with this entry. What it measured is written down instead: fifteen of the sixteen answer honestly, and on the sixteenth posture scan exits 0 with the headline "0 attack paths from 1 exposures; top: no path from an exposure to a node or to API rights" over a selectorless LoadBalancer Service and a privileged pod that mounts the host root -- a zero that is the absence of a resolution, not the absence of a path. The Service is named on the next line and in notRead and --require-complete exits 5, so the honest answer is reachable; the headline and the summary field are not fixed here and are recorded as an open defect in the case itself.
  • The checks that verify everything else could not see six rows of their own table or fifteen jobs of their own CI (G372). tests/test_guidance.py resolved the citations of 329 of the 335 rows in docs/guidance.md: its row expression needed three columns and a two- or three-digit id, so the six rows written in two columns -- G279, G319, G320, G321, G322, G325, the first of them citing 69 tests -- were dropped before any citation was resolved, and a four-digit G1000 would have been dropped the same way once the table reached it. One reader, tests/guidance_table.py, now matches a row by its id at any length and takes the citations from everything after the id whatever the column shape; all 335 rows are read and every citation in them resolves. No guidance row's text changed. Separately, fifteen jobs in release.yml, brew.yml, codeql.yml, scorecard.yml, risk-review.yml and db-image.yml carried no timeout-minutes, so a hang there read as "still running" for GitHub's six hours instead of as a failure; each now has a budget sized off its slowest successful run of the last ten, and the timeout check walks every workflow file rather than ci.yml alone. No job's name, steps or required status changed.
  • One question about a rule's evidence had two half-answers, and seven rules still stated or excused an absence a refusal explains (G369). The kinds a rule reads without judging them were declared twice -- context= on eight rules, asked only whether such an object was refused, and _PARTNER_KINDS on ten, asked only whether one was malformed -- and the newer guard ran only when the kind was absent from the whole input, so one readable object answered for a refused one beside it. On a bundle holding a readable PodDisruptionBudget and a refused policy.k8s.io/v1 one whose minAvailable: 3 on three replicas is what KN-C009 exists to catch, KN-C009 was reported checked-and-clean. And KN-C016 said "no ResourceQuota in this bundle" beside a refused ResourceQuota, KN-C008 "No PodDisruptionBudget selects this workload" beside a refused PDB that selects it, KN-C015 "no document in this bundle defines" the claim beside a refused claim of that name, KN-ST001 "neither defined in this bundle" beside a refused StorageClass of that name, KN-C007 reported a single replica a refused HorizontalPodAutoscaler would have excused, KN-S014 reported a pod for mounting a token a refused ServiceAccount opts it out of, and KN-S031 explained a claim as if the namespace were managed elsewhere. There is now one declaration, checked in one place against both predicates, and each rule declines with the refusal phrase and names the object -- per namespace or per named object, so a refusal in prod does not blind the rule in staging. KN-S031's HIGH finding still fires and is recorded as partly declined instead. A kind that genuinely is absent still fires its finding and is still reported absent, and a readable object of that kind is still read and the rule still clean.
  • policy reach answered DENIED from an AdminNetworkPolicy, BaselineAdminNetworkPolicy or ClusterNetworkPolicy in somebody else's API group, and audit could not refuse a ClusterNetworkPolicy at all (G371). G365 fixed this for NetworkPolicy and named the gap. The three cluster-scoped tiers have their own collectors: one matched kind alone and never read apiVersion, the other accepted any apiVersion whose version part ended /v1alpha2, so a document of one of those kinds under evil.example.com/v1alpha2 was evaluated as the Kubernetes kind of that name and reported as the policy blocking the traffic. refusal also knew no API group for ClusterNetworkPolicy, which meant nothing of that kind could be refused anywhere: audit over one under a foreign group exited 0 with an empty notAudited and no verdict, so an unrecognised shape read as an absent one, while the same mistake in an AdminNetworkPolicy was refused. Such a document is now named with the audit's own reason and the answer is undetermined in audit, policy reach and check_network_policy alike. A policy under the group kube-noob reads the kind under, and one with no apiVersion at all as an in-cluster list's items carry, is read and decides exactly as before.
  • A refused kind is not an absent kind (G368). On a bundle holding one projectcalico.org/v3 NetworkPolicy, KN-N001 fired a MEDIUM finding reading "No NetworkPolicy for namespace prod in this bundle" -- about a bundle that holds one, which the same report names under notAudited -- and five rules recorded evaluated without networking.k8s.io NetworkPolicy, the record that reaches the control summary, the FedRAMP KSI page, OSCAL, drift, gate and the MCP tools as "the input held no such object". A rule that reasons from the absence of a context= kind now asks whether the input holds one nothing read, per namespace, and declines with the refusal phrase instead of stating the absence. A genuinely absent kind is still reported absent, and a readable NetworkPolicy still covers its namespace.
  • The CI gating condition in the documentation merged a gate that could not read its inputs (G367). gate.sh reports result and evidence separately and always has; the documented condition used only result. A gate at fail-on: never, or one given no --containerfile, --scanner-report or --sarif-report, exits 0 -- so it reports result: passed with evidence: incomplete, and a workflow written to the old advice merged it with three families of check never run. The advice now requires result == 'passed' && evidence == 'complete' for a blocking gate, and says that gating on result alone is for a non-blocking rollout that watches evidence. No behaviour changes: the wrapper's outputs are unchanged and are now held by tests that run it.
  • posture diff reported a critical finding as fixed when its object merely stopped being read (G366). The guard that keeps a lost read from reading as an improvement was a set of KINDS, so moving one Deployment to an apiVersion the rules refuse -- while another Deployment in the same input stayed readable -- left the kind out of notReadKinds and sent the refused object's findings into fixed: 15 of them on the fixture, KN-S001 among them, with undetermined empty and the report's own headline reading 15 fixed. The object was named under notAudited in the same report. A finding now moves to fixed only when the object it was raised against was read in the new snapshot; otherwise it is carried under findings.undetermined, and the same object-level test decides whether an object is removed. A finding whose object is still read and no longer violating is reported as fixed exactly as before.
  • policy reach answered DENIED from a Calico NetworkPolicy it read as a Kubernetes one (G365). policy reach and the MCP tool check_network_policy matched kind: NetworkPolicy without its API group, so a projectcalico.org/v3 NetworkPolicy -- a different kind with a different schema -- was evaluated as the built-in and reported as the policy blocking the traffic, while audit refused the same object. The reader now asks the audit's own refusal check: such a policy makes the answer undetermined and is named, and a workload under a refused apiVersion is no longer an endpoint. A NetworkPolicy under networking.k8s.io or extensions, or with no apiVersion, is evaluated as before.
  • The renderer conformance suite holds the mixture, and docs/report-ir.md records what the mixture shows (G364). Eight fixtures put clean objects beside one object nothing read, in every position and for every reason an object goes unread; the webhook adapter is sent every object rather than the first. Nothing in the product's output changes with this entry. What it measured is written down instead: on such an input the verdict is correctly withheld, and the counts beside it -- clean per control, "N rules evaluated", KSI clean, a snapshot's evaluated rules, posture diff's "fixed" -- are the same numbers the clean objects give alone. Those are not fixed yet; the document proposes the coverage ledger that fixes them once.
  • A container at a place the reader for its kind does not look was scored and passed (G360). For a kind kube-noob has a reader for, the detector that reports a container no rule read returned nothing without looking, and DeploymentConfig, InferenceService and the KubeRay kinds have no bundled schema to notice. A DeploymentConfig with a privileged container under template.Spec scored 92.0 to 96.0, gated passed and verified allow; so did an InferenceService with a privileged explainer.alibi (79.0) and a RayCluster or InferenceService with a pod spec at an unlisted path (80.0). This was not a way to run a privileged container: a current API server prunes or rejects each of those fields. What was wrong was the score and the pass. Such an object is now listed under notAudited with the path of the field and the reason holds a container where kube-noob does not read one in this kind: audit prints no score and exits 1, gate is incomplete, verify answers not-determined (exit 5), the webhook warns, and posture scan lists it under refused. The findings on the part of the object that was read are unchanged, and a notAudited exception accepts the row as it does any other. A core kind with an unknown field holding a container (a Deployment with template.Spec) was already failing on KN-V007 and now also reports the container as not audited, so the gate reads incomplete where it read failed; the exit code is 1 either way. Still scored: a workload with no pod spec anywhere, such as a DeploymentConfig with no template.
  • OSCAL reported objectives satisfied and red_team answered mitigated beside an object nothing had read (G363). Whenever one readable object sat beside an unaudited one, every rule ran clean on the readable one and both outputs took that for the input: a hardened Pod beside a privileged Tekton step gave 16 satisfied/pass objectives and two mitigated scenarios in a run the gate called incomplete. While a notAudited row is open, or a file was skipped in a way that forbids a verdict, no control is reported satisfied on clean rules alone and no scenario is mitigated; findings and exposed scenarios are reported as before, and an accepted exception restores the objectives.
  • A container past the 200th item of a list was never looked at (G362). In a kind with no reader the detector read the first 200 entries of each list, so an Argo Workflow with a privileged container in its 201st template scored 100.0. It reads every entry now. The same detector no longer raises TypeError on a mapping that has an integer key beside a string key.
  • A weekly release-ready check says whether there is anything to release and whether every check on main is green, because the website is built from the latest release and the cadence had no check on it (G361).
  • A deterministic verdict nobody can re-derive is a claim, not evidence (G310). kube-noob's product claim is a verdict that is the same for the same input, and until now the only way to believe it was to trust whoever ran it. audit --receipt PATH and gate --receipt PATH write one self-describing JSON document binding the schema, the kube-noob that wrote it, every input by SHA-256 (file by file, with the path as given and the path it resolved to), the identity and the meaning of every rule in the rule set, the settings that changed the verdict, the scope actually read and what was not read, and the verdict itself -- counts, score, the reason there is no verdict (verdict.undetermined), exit code, and every finding by Finding.fingerprint(). kube-noob receipt verify PATH re-runs those inputs and answers identical (exit 0), differs and names the fields (exit 1), or cannot be checked (exit 5).

Exit 5 is the answer worth building. A verifier that cannot get an input and reports a pass is worse than no verifier, so a missing input, an input whose digest moved, a receipt from another kube-noob, a rule set that no longer means the same thing, and a run that cannot be replayed from the files the receipt binds are each "cannot be checked" with the thing it could not get named. A changed input digest is never "differs": a different input is a different question, and reporting it as a changed verdict would invite a reader to take a fresh audit for a reproduction.

  • Rule meaning, not just rule id. A rule id is stable on purpose -- a baseline, an exception list and a SARIF alert history all key on it -- so a receipt cannot rest on it: "KN-S010 clean" is worthless if KN-S010's default severity, subject or wording changed between the two runs. The receipt records a meaning version per rule, derived from Rule.as_dict() (the rule's own declared title, stage, category, default severity, description, remediation, citations, RHACS policies and NIST controls) and never hand-maintained, plus one digest over all of them. receipt verify checks the tool version and that digest BEFORE it re-runs anything, because a receipt from another build cannot be reproduced here whatever the files say, and calling that "differs" would read as a regression in the code.
  • Determinism is the product, so the clock is not in the payload. payload is byte-identical for identical inputs; envelope, outside the hashed payload, holds the time it was taken and how long it took. A test writes two receipts minutes apart over the same files and asserts the payloads are byte-identical and the envelopes are not. The envelope records no hostname and no runner identity, with the fields present and null and the reason in their place: an unsigned document cannot attest to who produced it, so a self-asserted host in it would be a claim the document itself says it cannot support -- and a receipt is written to be forwarded.
  • Integrity, not authenticity, said in the document's own text. SHA-256 only. No signature, no key, no key management, and cryptography is still not a dependency -- no new dependency at all. integrity.statement says that the receipt proves the same inputs give the same verdict and proves nothing about who produced it, and a test holds that sentence. A signer is an adapter that would wrap this document and sign the payload digest; the seam is left clean and nothing is built towards a key.
  • A file added since is caught, not reproduced. Per-input digests cannot see a file ADDED to a directory after the receipt was written: the receipt names what it read, the replay reads those files, both agree, and audit -f dir/ today would answer differently. The receipt carries one digest over the whole input set and the replay re-expands the -f argument the way the command does, so that is "cannot be checked" with the reason, which is a skipped input reading as complete in its own right.
  • What a receipt does not claim. A gate run that also read a Containerfile, a scanner report, a SARIF report, a VEX document, a KEV catalogue or a rendered Helm chart records replay.supported: false with the reason: those verdicts rest on a build context, on helm, or on the local advisory data in the data directory, none of which is a file the receipt binds. Standard input is bound by hash and is "cannot be checked" too -- its bytes are not a file to read again. The score and the reason there is no verdict are not the receipt's own judgement: Report.undetermined() (G272) is the one predicate, it reaches the receipt already serialised as summary.verdict: undetermined with summary.verdictReason (G281), and the receipt records it as verdict.undetermined. The exit code follows from it by the published rule -- an undetermined report is 1 whatever --fail-on says, because 1 means a verdict was reached and it is not a pass while 5 means none was reached at all (AGENTS.md, "1 and 5 are not two answers to the same question") -- and receipt.audit_exit_code is the single place the CLI, the receipt and the MCP verifier all read it from, as receipt.gate_exit_code is for the gate (G359). The three records behind an undetermined report travel in the receipt's scope (declinedRules, notEvaluatedRules, notAudited), and a gate receipt also records gate.verdict_word, which is incomplete and never passed there.
  • Finding.fingerprint() is now kube_noob.audit.model.fingerprint_for(rule_id, resource, locator) with the method calling it, so the receipt fingerprints the gate's finding dicts -- which carry CVE and SAST results beside audit rules -- from the one definition instead of a second copy of the key. The identity itself is unchanged.
  • The MCP server gains one tool, verify_receipt (57 tools), offered under the review and compliance tool profiles. It takes the receipt and the manifests as text, because the server reads no path on the caller's machine; that bounds what it can check and the answer says so. Its input model is the only one on the server that does not strip white space: a receipt binds bytes, and trimming a trailing newline before hashing would turn every honest check into "the input changed".

  • A receipt read the verdict from a key the report no longer writes, so it would have re-opened the false pass G272 closed (G359). The receipt's rule is that it records the verdict it was given and never re-judges it, and it kept that rule against a report shape that moved under it. receipt.audit_exit_code -- the one place audit takes its exit code from -- read summary.scoreWithheld; G281 retired that name for summary.verdict: undetermined with summary.verdictReason, the key was then simply absent, and an absent key read as "nothing withheld". Merged as written, an undetermined audit with nothing at --fail-on exited 0, with or without --receipt, and its receipt recorded exit 0 and a null score with no reason. It now reads the two keys the report writes; a report that says undetermined and gives no reason is still exit 1. The gate had the same drift in three places. G325 gave gate a second way to exit 5 (a file the walk declined to read as a manifest) in a branch that ran after the receipt was written, so that run left a receipt saying 1. receipt verify recomputed the gate's exit code with two lines of its own that knew one of the two reasons, and did not pass --skip-unreadable on to the replay, so a faithful receipt of a waived run came back DIFFERS. And the receipt recorded the audit's undetermined alone as the reason, so a gate incomplete for a file (G340) said incomplete with no reason at all. One function, receipt.gate_exit_code, is now what the command returns, the receipt records and the replay recomputes, read off open_unread, the new blocked_manifests and passed; the reason is model.incomplete_reason, the reader every gate renderer already uses. The receipt's field is renamed verdict.scoreWithheld -> verdict.undetermined, before any release carried it: bringing back under a new document the name G281 removed as a second way of saying one thing would have been the same defect again. A gate receipt also wrote assumptions: [] whatever the gate had assumed and no count of the findings an exception removed; both are read off the report now. The receipt is a view of the verdict, so tests/test_renderer_conformance.py holds both documents to its two predicates (receipt.audit, receipt.gate: twenty adapters), and against the code as it was four cells of that matrix fail. verify_receipt is exempted there with what it still owes stated. Two sentences in docs/use-cases.md and docs/architecture.md still described summary.scoreWithheld and are corrected.

  • site-sync fails when the website's own last deploy failed, not only when the published site is behind a release: twelve consecutive deploy failures were invisible because nothing had moved (G358).

  • brew install kube-noob serves the version this repository is, and the release workflow stops pushing the formula at a protected branch and reporting success over a branch nobody merged (G357).

0.10.0 - 2026-11-23

  • A rerun of the release workflow after a partial PyPI upload is a no-op instead of a failure that strands the tag with no release page and no krew archive (G345).
  • kube-noob.com is published as soon as a release exists instead of after the 2-hour video render, and asked again once the videos are attached (G356).
  • The Agent Skill's install hint is generated from plugin/kube-noob/uv.lock instead of copied by hand, so a dependency bump can no longer leave a stale version behind two slow test legs (G349).
  • Commands that read a directory dropped the files they could not read (G350). posture scan, posture reach, posture snapshot, policy reach, vuln threat and protocol read -f DIR through a helper that kept the readable files and discarded the list of the rest, so posture scan -f k8s/ --fail-above 30 --require-complete exited 0 beside a file YAML could not parse. They now exit 5 naming each file, as the same file named on its own always was refused.
  • audit -f DIR scored 100.0 over a directory holding a manifest it never opened (G351). The audit's walk reads .yaml and .yml; a privileged Pod in pod.json beside a clean ok.yaml exited 0 where gate exited 5 on the same directory. The gate's decision about an unopened file is now one function both walks ask. The file is an unreadable row: exit 5, or --skip-unreadable to audit the rest with it still named. A JSON file that is not a manifest stays silent.
  • The gate passed a directory holding a JSON manifest YAML cannot parse (G352). JSON indented with tabs, or written as a stream of objects, is valid for kubectl apply -f and is not YAML, and the gate asked only the YAML parser whether an unopened file was a manifest. Such a pod.json now makes the gate incomplete, exit 5, like any other manifest the walk did not open.
  • posture scan --require-complete did nothing without --fail-above (G353). It exited 0 on an object the scan itself listed under refused. It now exits 5 on anything under notRead, with or without a score threshold.
  • verify -f DIR allowed a manifest under a chart's templates/ that kubectl would send (G354). It took the gate's "a Helm template" for a plain manifest and listed the file under files as checked. A file there that is a manifest as it stands is now audited; a template that is not yet YAML is still skipped. gate is unchanged.
  • Three more container shapes were read by no rule and reported clean (G355). An Argo templates[].script, a Tekton stepTemplate and a KubeVirt containerDisk are now reported as not audited, which withholds the verdict, as G333 did for steps, sidecars and container. An operator CR that declares only spec.image is still not detected.
  • scripts/check.sh run from a git worktree tested the main checkout instead (G346). The editable install records one checkout, every worktree shares it, and check.sh set no import root: it collected the worktree's tests and ran them against the other tree's modules. On a branch whose fix was correct that read 14 failed, 8567 passed, 13 of them the branch's own new tests failing as they fail without the fix -- a gate failing while the code is right, which is G264 in a mirror and costs the same trust. check.sh now resolves its own root (through symlinks, from any directory), exports an absolute PYTHONPATH, prints which tree it is testing, and refuses to run any gate when kube_noob imports from anywhere else, naming both paths. scripts/check.sh --where prints that and stops. Steps that drive a built artifact (the wheel's venv, the air-gap checks, the plugin's locked environment) run without the import root, since with it they would have tested src/ and called it the wheel. Also: check.sh ran mypy src where CI runs bare mypy; it now runs CI's command, and a test holds the two together.
  • A row or a changelog bullet that a union merge wrote twice is now named (G347). merge=union cannot conflict, so it cannot report that both sides carried the same line. tests/test_guidance.py refused a duplicate id only for rows in the three-column shape -- six rows are in two columns, and a repeat of one of them passed -- and said duplicate guidance ids without naming one; nothing checked CHANGELOG.md. tests/test_append_only_duplicates.py names every G<n> with more than one row and every bullet in the unreleased section that appears twice.
  • Every job in ci.yml has a timeout-minutes (G348). Only videos had one; test and ten others ran to GitHub's six-hour default, with one ci workflow running at a time. test gets 90 minutes against a slowest green leg of 59.1 over the last 30 runs; jobs without a cluster get 10, jobs with one get 20. The reasoning is in the workflow, and a test fails on a job without a budget or a test budget within a quarter of, or more than twice, the slowest green run.

  • kube-noob fix rewrote somebody else's custom resource into a broken built-in (G344). _fix_api_version rewrote any apiVersion whose kind it recognised, so a kind this tool models under a group it does not serve was read as a typo in a built-in. A Calico projectcalico.org/v3 NetworkPolicy, whose spec is a selector expression and an ingress of actions, came back as networking.k8s.io/v1; Knative's serving.knative.dev/v1 Service became core v1; Volcano's Job became batch/v1; and the Kubernetes documentation's own stable.example.com/v1 CronJob example became batch/v1. Each was a valid manifest turned into a broken one, and through protocol -- where apply_fixes is the default -- the broken core Service came back carrying a new HIGH finding of its own, the tool manufacturing the problem it then reports. The audit had this right all along: refusal says the apiVersion names a group it does not know that kind under, and withholds the verdict. So the reading half and the writing half of one binary disagreed about the same object, and the half with no guard was the one that edits files. The rewrite now asks that same refusal first and, when it answers, writes a manual line naming the reason and both readings -- the served apiVersion if this is a typo, nothing to change if it is a custom resource of that name -- rather than picking one. The guard is narrowed to a group no rule in this tool reads any kind under, read off the same table the audit uses, so a typo in the group of a real kind is still corrected: apps.openshift.io/v1 Route becomes route.openshift.io/v1 as before, because apps.openshift.io is a group kube-noob serves and the spec survives the rewrite. A wrong version inside a served group (apps/v3 Deployment, networking.k8s.io/v1beta9 NetworkPolicy, extensions/v1beta1 Ingress) is unchanged too.

  • One conformance suite for every renderer of the audit report, instead of an eighth one-renderer fix (G338). Seven renderers of the same report were each found, separately, to drop the half of the answer that says what was not evaluated -- the two SARIF builders, GitLab code quality, OSCAL, the admission webhook, the CLI's verify and three MCP tools -- and each fix taught one renderer one lesson, so the next renderer started from zero. tests/test_renderer_conformance.py reads the renderers out of the source with ast (the public functions of gate, oscal, admission and report_html, and every MCP tool whose input carries manifests) and holds each to one assertion over one fixture set: on a report whose verdict is withheld, the output says so AND its real consumer cannot read it as clean. Both halves, because two of the seven defects ended with the gap stated in prose a consumer does not parse beside a field it does. The predicates are written the way each consumer reads: an empty or findings-only runs[].results for SARIF, [] for GitLab, a satisfied/pass objective for OSCAL, allowed: true with no warning for the webhook, a clean masthead with no limits section for the Markdown and text views. A rule with no subject in the input is outside both predicates, so a renderer does not report a gap on every run. A renderer with no adapter and no stated exemption fails the suite, which is what makes renderer eight correct by construction. Putting them side by side found four defects nobody had tripped over, each recorded as a strict xfail so it fails the day it is fixed: red_team answers RT-NODE-CRASH: mitigated with empty evidence for an object no rule read, while RT-PRIVESC in the same answer answers undecided; the gate's incomplete predicate counts a file the walk declined to read and not a file it could not read, so gate.markdown writes "## kube-noob gate passed" for a run with an unreadable file in it; OSCAL reports objectives satisfied on that same run; and the HTML page and the OSCAL remark lose the file's name, both reading path from rows a gate report keys on file. No renderer is at fault for the Tekton Task with a privileged docker:24-dind step -- the gate reports that input as a pass, the detector is a separate change, and the fixture is checked against the report rather than declared, so the day that lands every renderer is held to it.

  • The gate reported passed for a run in which a file could not be read (G340). gate -f DIR exits 5 for a file YAML cannot parse, and the report the same run wrote said passed: true, incomplete: false: the Markdown summary read "gate passed" without naming the file and nothing was annotated on the pull request. incomplete counted the files the directory walk declined to open (G325) and never the ones it could not read. It does now, decided once in gate.run, so the headline, the HTML masthead, the annotations and the SARIF invocation's properties.incomplete all follow. --skip-unreadable still decides on the files that were read and still exits 0 or 1; each row it accepted carries acceptedBy, and the summary and the annotations now name the file under the flag too, where before they did not mention it.
  • The HTML report and the OSCAL document lost the name of a file that was not read (G341). Both read path from rows a gate report keys on file, so the page said "File not read." beside an empty name and the assessment-results remark said "could not be read and were skipped: ?.". All four renderers of those rows now read them through one function.
  • red_team answered mitigated on empty evidence for an object the audit had refused (G342). A privileged Deployment under apps.k8s.io/v1 came back RT-NODE-CRASH: mitigated and RT-ZONE-OUTAGE: mitigated with evidence: [], and three scenarios that are exposed for the same object under apps/v1 were missing from the answer. Every scenario that rests on an object nothing read is now undecided and names the object; a rule whose subject is simply absent from the input still does not withhold mitigated. Reached through the red_team MCP tool and kube-noob redteam.

  • OSCAL reported objectives satisfied on a run with a file that was never read (G343). G335's exclusion covered a rule that reached no verdict and not a file no rule ever met. A control evidenced only by clean rules is now excluded while a file is unread and nobody accepted that; findings are reported as before. audit --skip-unreadable marks the rows it accepted with acceptedBy.

  • OSCAL reported control objectives satisfied on partial evidence (G335). On a report the gate itself called undetermined, three objectives read satisfied/pass -- CM-6 on 6 clean rules with 27 that reached no verdict. The gap was stated in the finding's description, which a GRC tool does not parse while it reads status.state. A control whose evidence was not gathered is now excluded rather than reported: not-satisfied would assert the objective is unmet, which partial evidence does not say either, and this document already excluded controls no rule ran over at all. A rule that did not run because its subject is absent from the input ("no Secret in the input") is scope rather than a gap, so satisfied stays reachable -- a clean input reports 17 satisfied objectives where counting absent subjects as gaps left 1 of 23. A rule whose reason is not recorded counts as a gap. A control with a finding, a withheld rule or an accepted exception is never excluded. Per-control coverage counts are now emitted as OSCAL props as well, so what stands behind an objective is machine-readable. Measured on a Deployment-only directory: 11 of 31 controls excluded and 6 satisfied, where before this change it was 10 and 7 -- exactly one control moves, CM-2, which read satisfied on 1 clean rule with 1 that reached no verdict.

  • gate -f on a directory passed it while never opening some of its manifests (G332). The walk kept .yaml and .yml and did not enter dot-directories, and a file outside that was recorded nowhere -- not in files, not in skipped, not in unreadable. A directory holding pod.json (privileged, hostPID) and .hidden/pod.yaml (privileged, hostNetwork) answered exit 0, passed: true, skipped: [], while audit -f on the same directory reported eleven findings. skipped: [] was a false statement, not a thin one. The walk now records any file it declined to open that demonstrably holds a manifest -- it is opened and looked at for a document carrying both apiVersion and kind, because deciding from the extension would record .github/workflows/ci.yml and hold every real gate run incomplete -- and that forbids the verdict: the gate reads "incomplete", exit 5, and names each file with the rule that excluded it and what to pass instead.
