  • Video numbers come from the code: demos/video/facts.py fills every count, score and line number in the scripts when a video is built, and records the terminal lines (the CLI audit, Claude's audit and fix tool calls, the sidecar session from the CI transcript). The published videos had drifted: 23 MCP tools and a 68 MiB peak in the sidecar video (0.6.0: 41 tools, 92 MiB), findings at lines 21, 23, 24 in the Claude video (23, 25, 26), "sixty rules" (61), "61 real questions" (63), "4 safe fixes" (5). tests/test_video_facts.py fails a script that types a number, names a rule the audit does not find, or uses a fact that does not exist.
  • videos.json is checked against demos/video/stage/videos.schema.json before any voice is spoken.
  • Sound: every mix is normalised to the brand loudness before encoding (one linear gain, plus an oversampled true-peak limiter only when the gain needs it; the single loudnorm pass had left the teaser at -17.6 LUFS), and each finished video is measured (demos/video/loudness.py): -16 LUFS within 0.5 LU, true peak at most -1.5 dBTP, audio as long as the picture, or the build fails.
  • Captions: every video gets a WebVTT file from its voice-over timings (two lines of 42 characters, timed to the voice), for the website, YouTube and LinkedIn. The walkthrough render also writes youtube-description.txt with its numbers filled and the chapters at the real scene times (the script's had drifted by up to 8 seconds); its end card now lasts 12 seconds, long enough to be a YouTube chapter and carry an end screen.
  • Every release gets its videos (.github/workflows/videos.yml, called by release.yml after the release exists, or run by hand for an existing tag): 4K masters and 1080p copies of the walkthrough and the three animated videos, WebVTT captions, poster frames, the YouTube thumbnail and description, and SHA256SUMS-videos.txt, rendered from the tag's own code with the sidecar transcript its CI run recorded, each file measured before it is attached.
  • CI checks the videos on every change in a few minutes (videos job): the scripts, every fact, the layout rules on every frame and stills of every scene, with --engine silent (no voice model; the same timing). Playwright is pinned in demos/video/package-lock.json.
  • tests/test_guidance.py checks rows G100 and later too (it matched only two-digit ids).
  • Brand review: the social preview, LinkedIn banner, post and profile images are rendered in the bundled fonts and the brand colours (docs/brand/render.js through brand.js) and carry kube-noob.com; the banner no longer says "cited" or "pipx install" and the post no longer says "60 audit rules" (61, and a static image now carries no count). BRAND.md names the roadmap's headline under test ("The ground truth your AI SRE checks against"). The README's trademark line names every product it mentions, and tests/test_brand.py checks its counts (rules, playbooks, red-team scenarios, tracks and lessons, protocol steps) against the code.
  • Video red-team fixes:
  • The walkthrough no longer says every finding carries an RHACS policy; only the findings of rules that map to one do.
  • Captions cut a clause that is too long for one cue at the word nearest its middle and balance the two lines, so no word is left alone on screen ("fixes." had flashed by for 0.4 s). Every cue stays on screen for at least 0.8 s, and the check no longer exempts short text.
  • The sidecar memory limit is read from the container's cgroup (memory.max) instead of being typed in the recording script, and facts.py refuses a transcript whose limit differs from the manifest's, or whose pod port was not shown closed.
  • The mascot's flame no longer covers the hyphen of "kube-noob" in the sidecar video, and capture.js --check now fails any frame where a rocket or its flame covers a label.
  • Terminal text, the memory gauge and the shapes in the animated videos use only brand tokens. The brand test also refuses an rgb copy of a token. The DNS label no longer shows the kind-only port 53.
  • Cut recordings say so on screen ("… 8 more lines", "… 12 medium and low findings…").
  • demos/ai-video-prompts.md now says word for word what the rendered videos say, with a test that keeps it so. The answer Claude gives in the MCP video is checked against the example and the audit.
  • audit.high counts high findings only, as the CLI header does; audit.critical is a fact of its own.
  • The release videos workflow (videos.yml):
    • attaches every video that rendered and still fails the run when a leg did not;
    • renders three of the four videos even without a sidecar transcript;
    • takes the transcript from any ci run whose sidecar step passed, even when another job failed;
    • attaches the transcript to the release, so a later re-render does not depend on a 90-day artifact;
    • refuses a transcript of another version;
    • reads the facts from the tag's checkout when pipeline_ref is set.
  • The loudness normaliser keeps the whole true-peak headroom for the AAC encode.
  • Second red-team pass:
    • A word wider than a caption line is refused with the reason instead of crashing the wrapping.
    • A short clause after a long one joins it, and a span too short to read is refused as such.
    • Memory limits are read as Kubernetes quantities (0.5Gi, 1Ti).
    • A critical finding shows as CRIT.
    • Check marks and iso shapes use brand tokens.
    • The release keeps a transcript only when the sidecar video rendered from it.
    • Every ci run checks the fresh sidecar transcript with facts.py, including the version.

  • The KN-S022 fixture puts the cr8escape payload in the sysctl value, as the advisory describes; the API server refused the old one (payload in the name) before admission, so the policy test on kind could not see the policy. The policy test creates every namespace its fixtures name (ingress-nginx for the controller rules).

  • Video redesign (from a review of the published cuts: mostly empty terminals with small text, the same laptop shot four times, and no visual payoff):

  • The walkthrough puts the chapter's point in a rail beside a terminal sized to what it shows. The font is the largest that fits (22 to 36 px at 1080p). The lines to look at light up once the output is printed: findings, scores, verdicts and read-only steps. A slow push-in keeps every frame moving, and commands type fast, so no frame holds an empty box. Chapter cards become big numbers with progress dots over the brand aurora.
  • The MCP video gets a payoff scene, from the same audit the facts use. The example's YAML is shown with each finding lighting its line and a ring counting to the score. Then the fix's diff appears, the fixed lines turn green, the ring climbs to the score after the fixes, and the changes left for a person are named. The MCP terminal now uses 31 px type in a box as tall as its lines.
  • The voice says "2 a.m." as "2 AM": Kokoro took the periods of "a.m." as sentence breaks ("two A. M.") and paused twice. RHACS is "RH ACS", because "R H A C S" made the A sound like "uh". tests/test_video_voice.py fails any spoken line that still has an abbreviation with periods or a lone spelled-out "A".
  • The mux cuts the sound to the picture's own length instead of using -shortest. On the walkthrough, -shortest dropped the last two frames (video 150.167 s against audio 150.233 s), and the loudness check refused the file.
  • corpus-refresh: a saved Jira copy that no longer decrypts (CORPUS_PASSPHRASE changed since it was saved) starts a new copy with a warning instead of failing the daily run ("bad decrypt" on 2026-10-29).

0.6.0 - 2026-10-29

  • New documents for the weakest technologies in the owner's by-technology file, written from each project's own docs by writers that never saw the test half, every claim checked against the cited lines: Helm template functions (range, toYaml and nindent, default and required, tpl, .Files, lookup, subchart values), Gatekeeper usage (Rego in ConstraintTemplates, audit results, spec.match, ExpansionTemplate for workloads, install on OpenShift) and kubectl output and scripting (jsonpath, custom-columns, --sort-by, --field-selector, logs, wait, exec, diff). Four more drafts are held back because blinded graders on real posts found them worse more often than better: Rook-Ceph operations, Rook-Ceph troubleshooting, Jobs/CronJobs and kustomize, and Kyverno usage. In a long pasted post (over 300 characters), the words that name a document's subject ("gatekeeper", "rego", "helm", "chart") no longer count toward the words the document must share with the post, because every post about that subject shares them. A typed question or a short post still counts every word. Measured on the owner's two answered files with two blinded graders per row:
  • the subject rule on the train half: 69 and 61 answers better, 25 and 25 worse;
  • the shipped change against the previous answers on the test half, every changed row not used for a decision (122): 54 and 53 better, 31 and 27 worse. The 132 test rows that decided which drafts ship are retired.
  • Reported-issue answers fell from 523 to 515, because the new documents explain words that had looked rare.
  • Golden: unchanged in scope (89 of 96); out of scope 22 of 23 (rosa-019 now abstains).
  • Red team of the 0.6.0 candidate (an independent agent that built and installed the wheel): no high findings, two medium, three low, all fixed. A must-gather namespace with a trailing newline ("shop\n") passed the name check, because Python's $ also matches before a final newline, and split a printed read-only step into two shell lines (the second any no-argument command, such as env); every validator in the package now ends with \Z, and a test fails on any new one anchored with $ (pydantic fields keep $, which its Rust engine reads as the end of the text). The documented known-issues 'could not find any suitable subnets for creating the ELB' example found nothing: an error copied word for word from an issue (seven words in a row, one of them rare) now matches it, with exactError: true; on the owner's 6,218 community rows no match changed. With no playbook matched, plain Kubernetes is told to paste kubectl output, not oc; plain Kubernetes and other clouds no longer get ROSA or OpenShift reading (a GKE PVC post cited the ROSA EFS doc), and the RWX fix starts with what any cloud needs (a file-storage class whose driver supports ReadWriteMany) before the AWS and ODF specifics: 4 community rows changed, all in that fix's wording. scripts/bump_version.py now moves the MAJOR.MINOR image tag in docs/install.md and the versions in docs/openshift-catalog.md.
  • A RHACS ticket (roxctl, Scanner, Central, the UI) that never says the cluster is OpenShift is no longer matched against the OpenShift release notes: the OCPBUGS items it got shared only words with it. Graders on the 10 community rows that changed: 10 better. Jira key recall unchanged.
  • Kyverno and Gatekeeper have no playbook of their own, so a question about the policy itself ("How do I exempt only this Job without editing the policy?") gets the document section on that mechanism first (the PolicyException section), then the policy explainer. Golden pol-004 now answers: in scope 89 of 96; no community row changed. A Kyverno ValidatingPolicy denial ("Policy require-team-label failed") now names its policy.
  • The RHACS admission playbook needs a denial: the product's name alone ("stackrox", "Red Hat Advanced Cluster Security") had named it on almost every RHACS ticket (roxctl and Scanner bugs, CI flakes, feature requests). RHEL, "Red Hat" or RHACS alone no longer make a cluster OpenShift for steps and causes (they still count for the bug matcher); RHOCP does. Golden unchanged (88 of 96). Blinded graders on the rows that changed: the RHACS change test half 60 rows (58 and 58 better, 1 and 1 worse), train half 77 (75 better, 0 worse); the cluster-context change test half 60 rows, mostly oc against kubectl (7 and 2 better, 4 and 3 worse), train half 66 (7 better, 4 worse), where the losses were OpenShift posts that only say RHOCP (now recognised) or name RHACM.
  • A policy denial whose question is about the policy itself ("Should I just edit restricted-v2?", "how do I make the message friendlier?", "what are my options?"), or whose explainer has no change to offer, gets the engine's own playbook first (ValidatingAdmissionPolicy, SecurityContextConstraints, Pod Security Admission, RHACS), with the policy explainer right after it; a question about what to change in the object keeps the explainer's specific change first. The owner delegated this call. Golden pol-002, pol-015 and rhacs-001 now answer: in scope 88 of 96; no community row changed (9 posts carry a policy denial, none of this kind).
  • Answers fit the post's infrastructure (from the community graders' notes). rosa steps need a ROSA platform or text that says ROSA (they had shown on EKS, GKE and minikube posts); OpenShift text gets the openshift-dns steps, including oc logs -n openshift-dns -c dns -l dns.operator.openshift.io/daemonset-dns=default, and plain Kubernetes the kube-system CoreDNS steps, never both; with nothing pointing at a cause, an AWS cause (EFS, EBS, security groups, cloud firewalls) is no guess for text about another cloud or self-run hardware, and the NFS mount cause and fix no longer read as EFS-only. An Init:CrashLoopBackOff gets its own cause, the init containers' status and kubectl logs <pod> -c <init-container> --previous. "timed out waiting for the condition" names the mount playbook only next to mount, attach or volume words (alone it is Helm's, kubeadm's and every wait loop's). More English words are never taken as a namespace name. Held-out set 79 to 80 of 114; golden unchanged. Blinded graders on the rows that changed: test half 44 rows (28 and 36 better, 1 and 1 worse: a Kubespray-on-RHEL post read as OpenShift), train half 50 (37 better, 2 worse); the timeout change 15 rows, 15 better.
  • Triage tuned on a training set and checked on real posts. Cause patterns for 11 playbooks were tuned on a new training set (tests/fixtures/triage-train, 171 questions written by an agent that saw only the causes; the held-out set is never tuned on): an image that still runs on old nodes points at a removed tag; a bare name in pull access denied for <name> points at a local image, not a private registry; Volume capabilities not supported at an access mode the class cannot serve; the kernel OOM killer and NotReady nodes at node-level OOM; caches and raised limits that only delay the kill at a leak; requests from a shared template next to low kubectl top usage at oversized requests; OVN at an EgressFirewall; "not expired" no longer counts as expiry. When nothing points at a cause, CrashLoopBackOff, image pull and OOM answers show their most common cause (the process exits right after start; a wrong or missing tag; a limit below the working set) instead of a guess from shared words. Symptoms in plain words now name a playbook when they are specific to the failure: a Service with no endpoints (and get endpoints tables showing <none>, notReadyAddresses), a pod that keeps restarting, a probe that keeps failing, a pod flipping between Ready and NotReady, a PVC that stays Pending, a pull secret that cannot be retrieved, a default-deny policy, permission denied on a mounted volume, RHACS blocking a deployment, NET::ERR_CERT_*, an unmarshalling error about UTF-8, Pending for lack of memory, no servers could be reached, a DNS server on port 53. Signals that two blinded graders found harmful on the community halves were removed before shipping: a probe mentioned in passing, "mount failed", SERVFAIL and other DNS words, pod-to-pod traffic (on feature requests, design questions, Docker posts and CNI or CSI bug reports they named a playbook with the wrong cause). Steps carry the names the input gives and never an English word: pod, PVC and namespace names come from command-shaped text (no more kubectl describe pod keeps or -n issue), and Service steps use the Service name and selector from the paste (kubectl get svc web, Selector: lines, {.spec.selector} output). Measured: training set 87 to 136 of 171 causes right; held-out set 63 to 79 of 114, no item lost; golden unchanged (85 of 96 in scope, 21 of 23 out of scope, regressed 0). Community test half, two blinded graders: 28 answers changed in content (15 and 17 better, 4 and 7 worse), the default causes changed 105 more (24 and 25 better, 1 and 2 worse), and 30 answered rows lost an English word in a name slot; the train half agreed (31 changed: 16 better, 7 worse; default causes 120 changed: 94 better, 5 worse).
  • Cause-linked triage: the 17 playbooks that answer most real posts (CrashLoopBackOff, image pull, pull secret, OOM, config errors, invalid UTF-8, pending resources, PVC pending, mounts, probes, Service endpoints, RHACS admission, eviction, DNS, NetworkPolicy, x509, ROSA HCP nodes) tie each root cause to the words that point at it, the read-only steps that confirm it and its fixes. The answer shows the cause the input points at, that cause's fix, and its steps first; an OpenShift- or ROSA-only cause is not chosen when no platform is given and the text never says OpenShift or ROSA, and such text gets kubectl steps. Owner report: "ImagePullBackOff when the pull secret sits in a different namespace from the pod." had shown the OpenShift internal-registry cause and the image-puller fix; it now shows the pod-namespace cause, kubectl create secret docker-registry ... && kubectl patch serviceaccount ..., and kubectl get secrets -A --field-selector metadata.name=<secret>. New steps and fixes: the Deployment's image, pull secrets and ServiceAccount for image pulls; DNS full Service names, upstream resolvers and cluster DNS health; cloud firewalls for blocked traffic; certificate subjectAltName; missing volume sources; the RHACS Collector pods and logs. Measured on a held-out set of 114 questions written by an agent that saw only each playbook's causes (tests/fixtures/triage, scripts/eval_triage.py, gated by tests/test_triage.py): the labelled cause is shown for 63 (was 50). On the community test half, an independent grader compared what users see (cause, fix, first steps) on 121 answered rows that changed: 78 better, 7 worse, 36 neither.

  • Documents that answer a pasted post are marked related reading (related: true); the generic gather steps (get pods, describe, events, logs) appear only when the text or the platform names a cluster, otherwise the answer says to paste the error, events or logs; a typed conceptual question ("What is the difference between Degraded and Available...") whose only playbook is a weak match (confidence below 0.4) gets the document section that explains it first when its heading shares two specific words. Golden ocp-016 now answers: in scope 85 of 96.

  • kube-noob must-gather analyze <dir|.tar|.tar.gz|.tgz>: opens a must-gather (or oc adm inspect output) safely and offline, and says what it holds: each gather image's directory with its version and timestamp, the resource files by group and resource, namespaces, pods and logs. Nothing is extracted to disk and nothing is run: archives are read as a stream; a member with an absolute path, .. or a NUL refuses the whole archive; links, devices and FIFOs are never followed or read; files named like Secrets are never read (oc adm inspect already elides their values; kube-noob does not open them at all); nested archives are listed, not opened. Caps bound the file count, the bytes processed (a gzip bomb stops at the cap), each file, what is kept in memory and the nesting depth; logs keep their last MiB. The layout was read from openshift/oc's must-gather and inspect source.

  • Answer quality, measured on 25,292 community questions (scripts/eval_questions.py, docs/evals/community-questions.md): an OpenShift release-note bug no longer answers questions about other platforms (5,274 such answers before, 29 after): with no platform given, diagnose, insight and the watch search the release notes only when the text says it is about OpenShift, and everyday English words in any inflection ("deployed", "following", "mirrors") no longer count as rare evidence. OpenShift and ROSA playbooks need OpenShift or ROSA words when no platform is given (an OpenShift playbook's specific patterns, such as nginx's mkdir() ... (13: Permission denied), still name it). New trigger phrases: a PVC stuck in words, DNS trouble in words, oc get co rows and *ControllerDegraded reasons. Documents are offered only when they share enough specific words with the question; forum posts that ask in their title or a sentence are read as questions; reported-issue findings in insight carry the issue summary as their title. A graded sample of 76 rows is in docs/evals/community-questions-grades.csv (ids and verdicts, no question text); --grades carries each verdict over while the row's answer is unchanged and lists the rows a change moved, so a tuning change is re-graded only where it changed an answer.
  • Second tuning round from the graded sample: new knowledge, each fact checked against the Kubernetes and OpenShift documentation, for listing a Deployment's pods by label selector, a kubelet that will not start (swap, cgroup driver, runtime socket, expired client certificate, and the API server's --kubelet-certificate-authority), etcd backup and restore (etcdctl/etcdutl, OpenShift's cluster-backup.sh), reaching an Ingress from outside and why an Ingress serves HTTP and HTTPS only, moving a Docker Compose app with Kompose, and a non-root container that cannot write its volume (fsGroup). On OpenShift a process cannot listen on a port below 1024: the arbitrary-UID playbook and doc now say so (listen EACCES on port 80). Documents are offered more carefully: with no platform given, a doc written only for OpenShift or ROSA needs the question to say OpenShift or ROSA (as the playbooks do); a doc can declare requires word groups in its front matter (the Compose page needs Compose and a Kubernetes word, the etcd page a backup word); a typed question that names something the knowledge has never seen needs a document sharing three of its words; and shared words are counted over the whole section, not the 700-character preview. Checked on 40 held-out rows by an independent grader (docs/evals/community-questions-heldout.csv): wrong answers 33 to 14; scripts/eval_questions.py takes --grades more than once.
  • New playbook DBG-ENTRYPOINT-NOT-FOUND: exec: "<cmd>": executable file not found in $PATH or stat ...: no such file or directory when a container is created means its command is not in the image (runc looks it up with Go's exec.LookPath at create time). New knowledge on kubeconfig credential plugins (exec, installHint, relative paths) for "getting credentials: exec: executable ... not found".
  • A question that names another product (Istio, Argo CD, Trivy, Terraform, Docker Compose...) is answered by a document only if that document mentions the product: a doc sharing Kubernetes words with an Istio question does not answer it. scripts/eval_questions.py scores rows whose CSV carries accepted answers (answer_text): the share of the answer's specific words in kube-noob's top finding, totalled per answer kind, without writing the answer text out; --data-dir evaluates with a prepared data dir (for example with the official docs corpus built).
  • Per-technology measurement: docs/evals/supported-technologies.md lists what kube-noob answers from for each supported technology (Kubernetes, OpenShift, ROSA HCP, Helm, RHACS, admission policy engines, ODF) and how well it answers, graded on a fixed test half of the owner's answered questions. scripts/eval_questions.py --split train|test evaluates one fixed half (by a hash of the row id) and reports results per technology. scripts/eval_golden.py runs the golden question set (tests/fixtures/golden/, original questions written from each technology's official documentation, with out-of-scope questions that must stay unanswered) and fails when an item that passed regresses; tests/test_golden.py runs it in CI. Test rows whose grading informed a change are listed in docs/evals/community-questions-retired.csv (ids and the change only) and --split test leaves them out (--retired names another file). The golden set holds 119 questions (Kubernetes, Helm 3, OpenShift, ROSA HCP, RHACS, admission policy engines) written by independent agents from the official docs at pinned commits and graded by other independent agents; the baseline is 7 of 96 in-scope questions answered by the top finding (Kubernetes 4 of 16) and 15 of 23 out-of-scope questions left unanswered, each miss with its gap recorded. The SCC playbook also matches "unable to validate against any security context constraint" when a forum mangled the line.
  • Helm usage knowledge (Helm had the lowest graded score, 2 of 20 helpful): a new cited document, helm-templates-values, from the Helm documentation: values precedence (-f and --set, right-most wins) and removing a default with null; why helm upgrade forgets earlier --set values and what --reuse-values, --reset-then-reuse-values and --reset-values do; quoting strings but not integers; . and $ inside range and with ("nil pointer evaluating interface {}"); index for keys with dashes; include | nindent; required; the checksum annotation that rolls pods on a ConfigMap change; rendering one template; and the Helm 3 changes (a release name or --generate-name, no --name, uninstall --keep-history, namespaced releases). It answers only questions that name Helm, a chart or values.yaml. The image-pull playbook now names an image built only on a workstation (minikube, a local Docker) with :latest or no tag, so imagePullPolicy defaults to Always, and proposes minikube image load or minikube docker-env with IfNotPresent or Never.
  • Golden gaps closed for Kubernetes and Helm, each checked against the official page the grader named (kubernetes/website, the kubectl and client-go source, the Helm 3 docs): new playbooks for an API server kubectl cannot reach (Unable to connect to the server: dial tcp ...: i/o timeout, The connection to the server ... was refused, and localhost:8080 meaning no kubeconfig; it no longer reads as a NetworkPolicy problem), a stalled rollout (exceeded its progress deadline), a PVC stuck in Terminating (the kubernetes.io/pvc-protection finalizer and the pods still using it), helm repo add on an existing name (--force-update), an upgrade blocked by removed APIs in the stored release (helm-mapkubeapis) and a template that renders invalid YAML (whitespace chomping, nindent). Existing playbooks gained the documented CrashLoopBackOff back-off (10 s doubling to 300 s, reset after 10 minutes), kubectl debug --target for images without a shell, the CoreDNS checks (resolv.conf, k8s-app=kube-dns pods, logs, EndpointSlices, the system:coredns ClusterRole), the kubeconfig left over from kubeadm reset, and --take-ownership for Helm adoption. New documents: kubeadm first-day problems (CoreDNS Pending until a CNI, malformed pair extra-args), Deployment rollouts (history, undo, revision limit), Helm CLI operations (--set escaping, OCI push, rollback, three-way merge, Helm's paths) and Helm chart authoring (crds/, hooks, library charts, template comments). Flux HelmRelease, Chart.js and AWS Lambda questions no longer get Helm or ROSA documents. Golden: Kubernetes 13 of 16 and Helm 13 of 16 (a fit: those items named the gaps); on the community test half, which the changes were not built from, 36 answers moved: 4 better, 2 worse, 30 neither. The reported-issue matcher and the ticket correlation now call a word rare when it is in at most 2 sections of the bundled docs (GENERAL_MAX_CHUNKS, a count), not 2% of them: as a share, the new documents had made more words rare and four rows that pointed at the right bug named an unrelated one. A count of documents was measured as well (a document's title is indexed with every section, so long documents make their title words look common): it made more words rare, and on the community test half 33 answers moved, 4 better and 24 worse (22 rows with no answer named an unrelated bug), so the section count stays and those rows are retired. helm repo list is classified as a read-only step (helm repo add, update and remove stay changes).
  • insight findings show the cause and fix that fit the input, not the first of the playbook's list: each cause and fix is scored by the words it shares with the pasted text, each word weighted by how rare it is across all playbook text (a pasted minikube ErrImagePull now shows the local-image cause and minikube image load, not a registry typo and an image pull secret). Playbook and policy findings carry a fix, printed by the CLI; a policy finding lists every change the requester can make (exceptions and new permissions stay with the policy owner). A Pod Security denial followed by a question in the same paste now reads every violated check, not only the first.
  • The reported-issue matcher counts each piece of evidence once: "item" and "items", or "openshift-etcd" and "etcd", were two of the three rare shared words a match needs. On the train half of the owner's answered questions 55 top answers changed; an independent grader found all 55 earlier answers wrong, and 10 of the rows now point at the bug the report itself names (the original of a clone). On the whole train half, reported-bug answers on posts that are not Red Hat bug reports fell from 234 to 134; recall of the exact key in the top 3, when indexed, went from 21 to 19 of 33 (two keys that were third). Checked on the test half, which the change was not tuned on: 52 top answers changed, all 52 earlier ones wrong, 12 better now (11 point at the clone's original), none worse.
  • Golden gaps closed for OpenShift, ROSA HCP, RHACS and the admission policy engines, drafted from the cited official pages (openshift-docs 4.22 and rhacs-docs-main, the stackrox source, kubernetes/website, the Kyverno and Gatekeeper sites) by agents that never saw the golden questions, then reviewed and verified line by line: 19 new playbooks (OpenShift build image quota, CNI not ready, OLM OperatorGroup, image prune registry errors, CRI-O storage wipe; ROSA OCM5011, OCM5010, ELB service-linked role, user-role not linked, expired rosa token, Karpenter discovery tag and CreateTags; RHACS break glass, webhook failure policy, Scanner registry and CVE-data warnings, Collector to Sensor, roxctl image check; ValidatingAdmissionPolicy denials), 14 new documents (OpenShift install and bootstrap, ClusterOperator conditions and stuck updates, etcd quota, pod security label sync, service serving certificates; ROSA HCP lifecycle and troubleshooting; RHACS enforcement, policy rules and scope, roxctl in CI, secured cluster troubleshooting; ValidatingAdmissionPolicy, Kyverno, Gatekeeper), and new sections for Pod Security (server-side dry run, enforce only on Pods) and SCCs (PSA and SCC together, openshift.io/required-scc, granting to the ServiceAccount). The Kyverno, Gatekeeper, SCC and VAP explainers tell the policy owner the documented options (PolicyException, enforcementAction: dryrun/warn, exemptions, messageExpression, [Warn, Audit]). An RHACS webhook that could not be called (timeout) is no longer read as a policy denial; oc adm prune without --confirm and aws ... describe-/get-/list- are read-only steps; a document can declare worksWith products it applies to without naming them (roxctl runs in any CI system); short product names in the named-product gate ("aro") must be whole words of a document, and Azure Red Hat OpenShift questions no longer get ROSA documents. Golden in-scope answers: 7 of 96 at the baseline, 72 of 96 now (a fit; OpenShift and ROSA items are asked with their platform). On the community test half, which none of these changes were built from: 62 answers moved, 8 better, 3 worse, 51 neither.
  • A prefixed name written bare matches: the tokenizer also keeps the hyphenated name after a slash (service.beta.openshift.io/serving-cert-secret-name gives serving-cert-secret-name, openshift-operator-lifecycle-manager/catalog-operator gives catalog-operator), and the bug matcher counts the prefixed and the bare name as one piece of evidence. A question that names AWS Security Hub gets no document. Golden set unchanged (72 of 96 in scope, 18 of 23 out of scope left unanswered). Train half: 4 answers moved (2 better, 1 worse, 1 neither); test half, not tuned on: 10 moved, 6 better, 2 worse, 2 neither (independent grader).
  • Questions about Falco or NeuVector get no document (golden rhacs-019 and rhacs-020 had drawn the RHACS default policies and the image document). Golden RHACS out of scope 4 of 4 left unanswered (was 2); community test half: 2 answers moved, both better (independent grader).
  • Helm: helm list showing few or no releases has its own section (Helm 3 keeps each release in its namespace, so after moving from Helm 2, or after switching context, use -n or -A), and the template-scope section names the template call without a dot and its unknown object type "nil" error (helm-www Named Templates and Changes since Helm 2). A paste whose last sentence asks is also read as a question, so a post that quotes a template call gets documents; "although" and "though" are everyday words, not unknown terms. Golden: Helm 15 of 16 (helm-006 and helm-010 now answer, a fit), and an independent regrade of every golden miss moved RHACS to 14 of 16; in scope 76 of 96. Community: train half 8 answers moved (3 better, 5 neither), test half 10 moved (5 better, 0 worse, 5 neither).
  • A # line inside a fenced code block (a shell or YAML comment) no longer starts a new document section: the SCC document's openshift.io/required-scc section had been cut in three at the # ... lines of its example, so its first part lacked the example, the "SCC must exist" rule and the check command. Golden ocp-011 and pol-018 now answer: in scope 78 of 96 (OpenShift 12, policy 10). Community: train half 3 answers moved (1 better, 2 neither); test half 7 moved (1 better, 2 worse, 4 neither: the two worse rows moved because one document has fewer, longer sections, which shifts every search score a little).
  • Documented answers for six more golden gaps, each from the cited page: the termination message and terminationMessagePolicy: FallbackToLogsOnError (kubernetes/website, Determine the Reason for Pod Failure), CPU limits throttle while memory limits are enforced reactively by OOM kills (Resource Management for Pods and Containers), helm install/upgrade --take-ownership (Helm 3 Advanced techniques), ROSA HCP and Classic compared (control plane, infra nodes, upgrades, EC2 footprint) and the Multi-Attach recovery after a node crash (oc delete pod <old_pod> --force=true --grace-period=0, the volume is released after six minutes) from the OpenShift docs, and a task-led heading for rolling a ValidatingAdmissionPolicy out as [Warn, Audit] before [Deny]. The pod-debugging, storage access mode and requests-and-limits documents now need a word of their subject in the question (requires): with the new sections, generic words such as container, volume and exit had drawn them to ECS, EBS and C questions. Golden in scope 84 of 96 (Helm 16, Kubernetes 15, policy 11, ROSA HCP 16). Community: train half 66 answers moved (38 better, 8 worse, 20 neither); test half 66 moved (32 better, 12 worse, 22 neither), independent grader; most of the better rows are unrelated documents no longer given.
  • The 19 documents that answered most community questions (the Kubernetes overview, RHACS, everyday errors, images, Ingress, kubelet, NetworkPolicy, TLS, Helm, label selectors, DNS, RBAC, scheduling, removed APIs, Services, Pod Security, learning paths, topology spread) now need a word of their subject in the question, and of Kubernetes where their own words are generic (image, certificate, label, service). Measured on the community halves, where these documents mostly answered posts about something else: on the test half, document answers fell from 1,032 to 633 rows. An independent grader compared every moved answer among the answered test rows (80: 60 better, 4 worse, 16 neither) and a random 100 of the other moved test rows (84 better, 3 worse, 13 neither); on the answered train rows 85 moved: 63 better, 3 worse, 19 neither. Golden unchanged in scope (84 of 96); an AWS IAM question no longer gets the image document, so out of scope 21 of 23 are left unanswered.
  • New playbook DBG-INVALID-UTF8 for grpc: error unmarshalling request: string field contains invalid UTF-8 (asked by the owner; kube-noob had answered it with generic gather steps only). Its causes come from public reports and say so: an environment variable from a Secret or ConfigMap that decodes to bytes that are not UTF-8 (often a value base64-encoded twice, or binary data), rejected by the container runtime with the pod in CreateContainerError, reported after nodes moved to containerd 2.2; and the RHACS Collector case (String field 'storage.ProcessSignal.args' contains invalid UTF-8 data, RHACS 4.5, ROX-26025). Fixes: plain text under stringData or base64 encoded exactly once under data, binary data as a mounted file. The read-only steps list a Secret's key names, never its values. Its document (k8s-invalid-utf8, citing the reports and the Kubernetes Secret and ConfigMap pages) answers only questions that name UTF-8. Golden unchanged. Community: train 6 answers moved (4 better, 2 worse), test 6 moved (3 better, 3 worse; a new document shifts every search score a little).
  • REST API beside MCP on the HTTP server (backlog I6): POST /v1/insight, /v1/diagnose, /v1/audit, /v1/known-issues, /v1/policy/reach, /v1/memory/match, /v1/must-gather and GET/POST /v1/reports, JSON in and out, the same tools, validation, sanitising, knowledgeAsOf and content-free audit line as MCP (via=rest in the audit log). Bearer token and body limit as for MCP; Host and Origin checks; posts must be application/json. GET /v1/openapi.json (and docs/api/openapi.json, generated by scripts/gen_openapi.py from the tools' models and kept equal to the code by a test) describes them; GET /v1 lists the routes this install offers. /v1/net/verify comes with net verify (N5).
  • MCP tool analyze_must_gather (backlog G5): the must-gather report for a path on the machine the server runs on, with the top findings (top) and, on request, the case draft as text. The local stdio server reads any absolute path you give; the HTTP server offers the tool only with mustGather.allowedDir ($KUBE_NOOB_MUST_GATHER_DIR, chart mustGather.pvc.claimName, mounted read-only at /srv/kube-noob/must-gather) and reads only paths that resolve inside it, links resolved first. The tool is not in the Claude plugin, which reads no files of yours.
  • must-gather analyze --report DIR [--case-draft] (backlog G3) writes report.md and report.json: the summary (version, update state, platform, nodes, operators, the OLM operators installed), every finding ranked with its evidence files, signals and read-only commands, the OpenShift known issues and Jira cache tickets that match each finding with their status and fix version for the detected version, approved team memory entries that match, the read-only next steps in order, what was read and skipped, and the knowledge date. case-draft.md is a support case with the facts filled in (environment, update history, evidence, matched bugs, suggested read-only checks) and a [FILL IN: ...] placeholder for everything only you know (problem statement, start time, change, frequency, impact, severity, steps taken); nothing is invented. Every string is redacted again on the way out and the must-gather's path is reduced to its file name. kube-noob demo must-gather walks through it offline in under a second. Go's monotonic clock suffix (m=+0.03) is dropped from must-gather timestamps. "too", "took", "long", "time", "times", "very", "much", "many" and "few" no longer count as rare words in known-issue matching (an etcd "apply request took too long" line matched a PTP issue on them; the recall eval is unchanged).
  • Code findings red team (SAST1): 16 reproduced findings fixed, each with a regression test (tests/test_code_redteam.py). Fail-open paths closed: an absolute -c/--containerfile path or context, the gate having no build context (--containerfile PATH[=IMAGE][,context=DIR][,target=STAGE] now, as in code report -c), RUN --mount=type=bind builds (the docker init Go and Rust templates), a UTF-8 byte order mark, a tab after an instruction or inside a flag, and <<WORD outside RUN, COPY and ADD each made shipped code "not shipped". Shipping that cannot be known (a path outside the source root, a path that is no repository file, a Containerfile that could not be read) now fails closed and says why, and SARIF notes reach the gate summary. COPY *.py no longer matches tests/x.py (Go filepath.Match), <Containerfile>.dockerignore takes precedence as in BuildKit, dependency lines skip <exclusions>, package.json scripts and lockfile requires maps, stage traversal and SARIF merging are linear (a 26-stage Containerfile hung the gate; 40,000 results took minutes), dependency patterns no longer backtrack, invalid .dockerignore classes are skipped instead of crashing, percent-decoded control characters and lone surrogates in SARIF are neutralised, and insight recognises SARIF without $schema.
  • must-gather analyze now says what is wrong (backlog G2), ranked by severity, each finding with the files it came from, its signals and read-only next steps: the cluster version (current, desired, channel, history; Failing, Available, Invalid, ReleaseAccepted, Upgradeable, RetrievedUpdates; an update that stopped partway), cluster operators (not Available, Degraded, Upgradeable=False), nodes (NotReady or no longer reporting, memory, disk and PID pressure, network unavailable, cordoned; control-plane nodes rank first), machine config pools (each *Degraded condition, degraded machines, updating, paused), OLM operators (Failed, Pending, Unknown; the copies OLM writes into every namespace are folded into the original), pods (container waiting reasons such as CrashLoopBackOff and ImagePullBackOff, OOM kills, Unschedulable, Evicted; replicas of one owner are one finding, with the last error line of the crashing container's log), warning events by reason (summed over count and series.count), and etcd log hints (apply request took too long, slow fdatasync, heartbeat on time, leader elections across terms). The platform (Infrastructure) and the installed operators are reported for the case draft (G3). Every finding feeds the signals that known-issue and memory matching use: the alert a condition implies (KubeNodeNotReady, ClusterOperatorDegraded, KubePodCrashLooping, etcdHighFsyncDurations), event and container reasons, components. --fail-on exits 1 at a severity. Field and value names were read from kubernetes/api, kubelet, openshift/api, operator-framework/api and etcd. Safety: YAML is parsed with libyaml after an event pre-scan that refuses aliases, more than 10 documents and nesting deeper than 64 (libyaml's composer crashes the process on 100,000 nested brackets; the pre-scan does not), every shown string is flattened and redacted, and a name that is not a valid Kubernetes name never reaches a next-step command.
  • kube-noob code report (and the MCP tool code_report, insight for a pasted SARIF, gate --sarif-report, and the Action, Tekton and GitLab input sarif-reports): what your SAST tools already found, where it ships and who runs it. kube-noob has no analysis engine of its own; it reads the SARIF 2.1.0 that Semgrep, Bandit, SpotBugs/FindSecBugs, ESLint and CodeQL write (shapes read from each tool's source: rules in CodeQL's query-pack extensions, SpotBugs' message arguments and hashed source-root bases, ESLint's absolute file URIs, Bandit's issue severity, Semgrep's CWE tags). The same file, line and CWE from several tools is one finding; suppressed, fixed (baseline) and non-failing results are excluded with the reason; lint and quality results are kept apart and never ranked. Each file is followed through the Containerfiles (multi-stage aware, .dockerignore honoured) into the images that ship it, copied or built, and with the manifests findings rank by exposure and privileges with their attack path; a finding in tests or tools no image ships is listed, not ranked. With scanner reports of the images (-r), each language-package vulnerability points at the dependency file and line to change (the lockfile the image copied, then requirements.txt, package.json, pom.xml, go.mod and the rest by name). Trivy, Grype and CycloneDX findings now record where in the image the package was found. A four-service fixture (five tools, 15 results) gives 8 findings, 3 merged across tools, the internet-facing SQL injection ranked first and the three dependency lines; 400 fuzzed SARIF documents never crash the reader.
  • kube-noob-admission (chart admission.enabled, kube-noob admission serve, capability tier A): a validating webhook that runs the audit on every created or updated object and returns each finding as an admission warning, so kubectl and oc print it. It denies nothing until a rule is listed in admission.enforce, then only that rule, and on an update only a finding the old object did not have. failurePolicy: Ignore and a 3-second timeout: a dead or slow webhook blocks nothing. It never mutates and fetches nothing; any error admits with a warning. The system namespaces, openshift-*, its own namespace, admission.excludedNamespaces, namespaces labelled kube-noob.io/admission=disabled, and Pods, ReplicaSets and Jobs a controller owns are skipped. Cross-object rules warn only with admission.lookups (a read-only, jittered background cache) and are never enforced. Exceptions use the audit's ExceptionList. The chart runs two replicas with a PodDisruptionBudget and soft anti-affinity; the serving certificate comes from the OpenShift service CA (default), cert-manager or your Secret, and the webhook restarts when it rotates. kube-noob admission review -f review.json evaluates one AdmissionReview offline. Tests: 1,500 fuzzed reviews never denied in warn mode, each enforceable rule denies only itself, p99 well under 50 ms; CI installs it on kind (warnings, fuzz and 50 requests per second from inside the cluster, enforce, scale to zero). kube-noob doctor lists tier A.
  • Vulnerability assessment, after an independent red team of vuln report, vuln threat and gate --scanner-report (each finding has a regression test in tests/test_vuln_redteam.py):
  • A Red Hat VEX statement for one module stream (nodejs:18) no longer decides a package from another (nodejs:20); when statements for one package disagree, the finding stays.
  • Two reports are one image only by digest, image id, or the same tag and RHEL release: two images tagged latest, or two unnamed reports, no longer share VEX scoping (a RHEL 9 statement could drop a RHEL 8 finding).
  • A vendor's own RPM (nginx.org's .el9.ngx build) is outside Red Hat VEX and keeps its finding.
  • A finding with no severity is graded by its CVSS score (medium without one) and a known-exploited one counts as at least high, in vuln report --fail-on and in the gate: neither passed before.
  • A CycloneDX report's own analysis (not_affected, resolved, false_positive) no longer clears its findings; --trust-report-vex (CLI, gate, MCP trust_report_vex, Action, Tekton and GitLab inputs) restores it.
  • Report text (an image or package name) can no longer add headings, links, images or table rows to the gate's Markdown summary, or line breaks to SARIF and logs; ids with trailing whitespace still match KEV.
  • Malformed VEX shapes are skipped instead of crashing; more VEX files than the limit is an error, not a silent cut.
  • --product (and the MCP products) must name a release (cpe:/o:redhat:enterprise_linux:9).
  • Every workload that runs a vulnerable image is counted, not the first 20.

  • Attack-path reachability is linear in the input (16,000 objects in under a second; it was cubic).

  • The Claude plugin no longer reads the kernel's FIPS flag (/proc/sys/crypto/fips_enabled): the plugin directory's scan flags reads outside the plugin's own folders, and it held 0.5.0 for it. kube-noob doctor and the service still report it; the plugin reports OpenSSL's FIPS mode and says the kernel flag was not read. A test keeps host paths out of the bundle.

  • kube-noob vuln report (and the MCP tool vuln_report, and insight for a pasted report): vulnerability findings from what scanners already produce. Trivy and Grype JSON, CycloneDX (as Trivy and as Grype write it) and RHACS image scans give one finding per package, installed version, CVE and fix version, the same in every format; SPDX 2 and CycloneDX SBOMs give packages that are matched against Red Hat VEX. Red Hat CSAF VEX (the redhat-vex corpus shard and --vex files) drops a finding Red Hat says does not affect the image's RHEL release, or that is fixed at or below the installed version (rpm's own version comparison, tested with rpm's vectors), and cites the statement, its justification and the document. A fixture image with 40 findings and 12 not-affected statements reports 28 in all six formats. With the CISA KEV catalog (--kev, CC0) and the manifests (-m), findings rank by base score x exposure x privileges x known exploitation with their attack path; the planted exposed, privileged, KEV-listed finding ranks first. --rhacs-image sha256:<digest> reads Central's scan (rhacs.enabled, GET /v1/images/<digest>, jittered). Parsers are bounded and hostile-tested.
  • kube-noob vuln threat (and the MCP tool threat_model): a STRIDE threat model per application from its manifests and the attack-path model: entry points, trust boundaries, data stores and identities, each threat with its status and evidence, the rules, generated admission policies and RHACS policies that mitigate it, and MITRE ATT&CK for Containers technique ids (Enterprise ATT&CK 20.0, with MITRE's attribution). A three-tier fixture gives a fixed, reviewed list of 20 threats; the output is deterministic and Secret objects are never read.
  • kube-noob gate --scanner-report <file> (with --vex and --kev): the images' vulnerabilities join the gate after VEX, ranked against the manifests, in SARIF and the summary; a fixable or known-exploited one at --fail-on fails the gate, an unfixable one is reported. The "vulnerability checks not run" note is gone when reports are given.
  • kube-noob build analytics --history <dir>: build and test analytics from a CI history on disk (one folder per run: the GitHub Actions run with its jobs and steps, JUnit XML reports, optional build logs). Flaky tests (passed and failed on the same commit, or failed then passed on a rerun within a run, as Surefire reports), tests that fail without being flaky, commits with a failed and a successful run, per-step mean, median, p90 and maximum with the trend of the newest half against the oldest, the slowest tests, and failure classes per week (from the build logs). A 200-run fixture history gives the exact flaky set and step times computed independently by its generator. JUnit with a DOCTYPE or entities is refused. kube-noob build history --github owner/repo -o <dir> writes the run folders from the GitHub Actions API (ci.githubEnabled; GET only, one host, no redirects, every attempt of every run, a random pause between requests, token from $GITHUB_TOKEN).
  • kube-noob gate: the CI gate before deploy. One command over manifests, rendered charts (--chart, findings point at the template file) and Containerfiles runs the audit (with --exceptions), says for each finding whether the generated admission policy would refuse it in enforce mode, lists open reported issues in the target OpenShift release that name a deployed kind, and fails at --fail-on (default high). It writes SARIF 2.1.0 (GitHub code scanning), a Markdown summary and GitLab code quality JSON; directory walks skip dot directories, Helm templates and YAML that is not a manifest, and list them. Shipped as a GitHub Action (uses: aashishmahajan/kube-noob@<tag>, dependencies pinned by hash, inputs passed through the environment), a Tekton Task (deploy/tekton/kube-noob-gate.yaml, read-only source workspace) and a GitLab CI template (deploy/gitlab/kube-noob-gate.gitlab-ci.yml, code quality report). CI runs all three: our chart passes, the bad fixtures fail. Vulnerability checks run with --scanner-report (above).
  • kube-noob build diagnose (and the MCP tool build_diagnose, and insight for a pasted build log): a failed GitHub Actions, Jenkins (Pipeline and freestyle), Tekton (tkn ... logs) or OpenShift build (oc logs build/...) log gives the failing step and why it is that step, the error line and its template, the cause class (dependency fetch, compile, test, image push, registry auth, quota, timeout, out of memory, permissions) with its evidence, reported issues and team memory for the error lines, and the fix for that CI system. 60 labelled logs in each system's format: the right class and step for all 60 (55 classes on the first run, before tuning; RT2 measures blind). Lines quoted from the log are redacted; hostile logs are bounded.
  • kube-noob build audit (and the MCP tool containerfile_audit, and insight for a pasted Containerfile): KN-B001 to KN-B008 check a Containerfile (base image by digest, non-root USER, credentials in ENV/ARG/LABEL/RUN or copied files, downloads piped into a shell, package caches, HEALTHCHECK or the workload's probes with --manifests, multi-stage builds), each with a good and a bad fixture; --context checks what COPY . . would take past .containerignore/.dockerignore; --image-layout reads the built image from an OCI layout on disk without extracting it (every blob checked against its digest) and finds credentials in its layers (KN-B009, including one a later layer deleted) and in its config and history (KN-B010). kube-noob explain KN-B003 works for these checks.
  • kube-noob rhacs compare (and the MCP tool rhacs_compare; rhacs_compare_live with rhacs.enabled): every policy in an RHACS export, or read from Central, gets an outcome with the reason: overlap (the kube-noob rules that check the same setting, with severity and scope differences), related (the same risk seen through image, runtime or API-event data), gap (a deploy-time setting kube-noob has no rule for, such as AppArmor or an egress NetworkPolicy), outside (image contents, runtime behaviour, API events, label conventions) or unexplained (criteria kube-noob does not know; exit 1). It also lists where the two disagree (RHACS flags Secrets passed as environment variables; kube-noob suggests secretKeyRef over literal values), default policies RHACS ships disabled that kube-noob checks, and the rules no policy covers. RHACS's 89 default policies map with none unexplained. Reading Central is GET-only, to rhacs.endpoint only, with no redirects, a jittered pause between requests, the token from $ROX_API_TOKEN, and results reused for 5 minutes; the chart's rhacs values wire the token Secret, Central's CA and egress to the Central pods.
  • Daily drift and policy report: kube-noob posture snapshot, posture diff and posture report, serve with posture.reportEnabled (one a day, jittered; chart posture.report.enabled, which needs cluster access, persistence and one replica) and the MCP tool posture_report. Snapshots hold hashes and names, never values; reports list objects added, removed and changed (with the changed fields), findings new and fixed, and open findings by namespace, owner and rule. Two snapshots of a fixture cluster give the exact report written by hand.
  • Exceptions as code (kube-noob.io/v1 ExceptionList): kube-noob exceptions check, audit --exceptions and policy generate --exceptions (MCP: exceptions on audit_manifests and policy_generate). Each entry names a rule, a scope, an owner, a reason and an expiry; expired, ownerless, unexplained, too-broad or far-future entries are refused with the reason and fail the gate; active entries hide only the findings they cover and become exemptions in the generated admission policies (with the ReplicaSets, Jobs and pods of the excepted workload).
  • kube-noob posture scan (and the MCP tools posture_scan and, with cluster access, cluster_posture_scan): attack paths from an exposure (Route, Ingress, LoadBalancer, NodePort, externalIPs) through the Service and the pods it selects to node access or the RBAC of the pod's ServiceAccount token, ranked by exposure x blast radius, each step with its evidence and a fix. Built-in roles (cluster-admin, admin, edit, view) are known by their documented meaning; aggregated ClusterRoles in a bundle are resolved; OpenShift SCCs count with the right to create workloads. --fail-above gates CI. On a kind cluster seeded with decoys, the planted path internet -> Route -> pod -> ServiceAccount -> get secrets ranks first, and every kubectl call is a read with none for Secrets. The read-only allowlist (and the chart's read Role) now includes Roles, RoleBindings, ClusterRoles, ClusterRoleBindings and DeploymentConfigs; Secrets stay out.
  • kube-noob policy generate (and the MCP tool policy_generate): admission policies from the audit rules, as ValidatingAdmissionPolicy + binding and/or Kyverno ValidatingPolicy (policies.kyverno.io/v1, the CEL policy type that replaces ClusterPolicy from Kyverno 1.19), in audit or enforce mode, for a platform. 39 of 61 rules on OpenShift (32 on Kubernetes, 40 on ROSA HCP) become policies named after the rule, with the fix and the source in the message; the other 21 are listed with the reason (they need other objects, or the API server already refuses them, or, for committed Secrets, belong in CI). A policy refuses exactly what the audit reports for that object at low severity or above: compared on 2,700 generated objects with cel-python, and applied to a kind cluster in CI (server-side dry-run of every rule's good and bad fixture, both engines). Platform namespaces and operators are exempt; calibrate rules skip controller-owned objects.
  • Every audit rule now ships a good and a bad fixture (tests/fixtures/rules/); the test suite fails when a rule has none or when the audit does not flag the bad one and pass the good one.
  • Fixed: KN-C004, KN-C005 and KN-C006 flagged ephemeral (debug) containers for missing resources, which the API does not allow on them.
  • CI: test failures and the live VEX check are reported as annotations (the runner log was not readable from the build loop). The live VEX step found that Red Hat's document for CVE-2023-44487 is larger than the 25 MB cap: it is reported as not read, as designed; the step now checks a second CVE.
  • Red team phase 1 (RT1, an independent reviewer; every finding is a regression test in tests/test_red_team_rt1.py):
  • Fixed (high): a Kubernetes RBAC denial whose user name carried shell syntax ($(...), quotes, backticks, ;, a newline) put it into the "read-only" oc auth can-i --as=... and grep steps of diagnose and insight; pasting one ran it. Names now go into a step only when they hold nothing a shell treats specially; otherwise the placeholder stays. A service account keeps its own namespace in --as=.
  • Fixed (high): insight took seconds per 10 KB on pastes with many blank lines (quadratic patterns, outside the time budget); every line pattern is now linear and long pastes are sampled at both ends: 2 MB in under 0.5 s.
  • Fixed: an API server Status response (what oc apply -o json prints when refused) crashed signal extraction; it is now read as the denial it carries. Fields that should be objects but are strings no longer break signals or a Jira sync.
  • Fixed: prompt-injection detection caught 8 of 50 new attacks and rewrote 47 of 55 lines of everyday operations text. Rewritten as two tiers (removed / flagged only) on a normalised copy with more languages and base64; now 48 of 50, and of the 55 operations lines 1 is altered (a remote badge image) and 8 are flagged. The kubenoob://knowledge/{doc} resource returned ingested text unsanitised; it is cleaned too. Emoji joiners and a leading byte-order mark are no longer reported.
  • Fixed: Jira anonymisation kept a commenter's name in the record title and missed spelled-out, URL-encoded, full-width and suffix-less emails, international phone numbers, @handles, GitHub, GitLab, LinkedIn and Confluence profile links, the name in a quoted reply's "On ..., Name wrote:", a "Thanks, Name" closing, the line under a signature delimiter, names in other letter case or without accents, and "Last, First" display names. Copies already stored are re-scrubbed once on the next sync, offline. The jira-audit self-check now runs the anonymiser's own patterns, so anything they would still change is reported. Image digests, package versions, @types/... and repository links are kept.
  • Fixed: a refused corpus import could leave a valid shard copied before a later one failed; all shards are checked before any is copied and they move into place together. The index reads only shards the corpus manifest lists.
  • Fixed: corpus history cloned its repositories back to back; clones are now spaced by a random pause.
  • Fixed: selftest --cases crashed on a deeply nested JSONL line; it is refused with the line number.
  • The recogniser, measured blind by the red team on 86 real-shaped pastes, had precision 0.94 and recall 0.64. It now reads get pods tables (also with colour codes and CRLF), scanner tables (Trivy, Grype, roxctl), tshark and tcpdump text with interfaces, GitHub Actions, Tekton and BuildKit logs, Prometheus /api/v1/alerts, alert notification text, access logs, lowercase Dockerfiles, SPDX tag-value, truncated YAML and Event JSON, must-gather output, a JSON paste with words before or logs after it, a question next to a paste, and Red Hat Jira keys; small talk is no longer a question. On the same set (now a regression set): 1.00 and 0.98.

  • Roadmap rewritten to the moat program (M1 to M12): signal-keyed matching, fixes-as-code memory, must-gather pre-flight, network truth, verifier hardening, integrations without AI, the kube-noob gate and governance. The trained-model track is cut; the grader and kube-noob train bench stay as an evaluation product; learner tracks, credits and brand videos are frozen. New docs/editions.md records where an enterprise line could fall (documentation only).

  • Capability tiers: probe (P), server-side dry-run (D), external writes (W) and gate forwarding (G) join read-only (R) as explicit switches, all off by default. doctor lists every tier, what it allows and what it needs, and reports contradictions (a probe without cluster access, a ticket write under the offline lock). Anything that changes something is a writer that cannot exist while its tier is off and needs a person's approval for exactly that action, unexpired and used once. The chart renders a probe Role and a dry-run Role only when enabled.

  • KN-S017 also flags workload creation and changes (pods, Deployments, Jobs...), ephemeral containers and PersistentVolume creation: each lets a subject act as any ServiceAccount in the namespace or reach the host (Kubernetes RBAC good practices).
  • kube-noob signals extracts the canonical names in an error: Alertmanager alert names (webhook payload or text), Kubernetes event reasons, ClusterOperator conditions with their reasons (YAML, JSON or oc get co), container waiting and terminated reasons, admission rules, components, and normalised error templates with a short hash. Vocabularies are copied from the upstream files that emit them (kubelet, scheduler, controllers, CRI-O, openshift/api) and cited in the code. Signals are the join key the next items use for known issues and memory.
  • Signal catalogue (kube_noob/data/signals.yaml, 74 entries): etcd, API server, node, workload, OpenShift platform (ClusterOperators, cluster version, machine config, ingress, OVN-Kubernetes) alerts plus event and container reasons, each with the phrases people use for it, search terms, components, playbooks and the upstream rule file it comes from. Where OpenShift names differ from upstream both are listed (etcdGRPCReadRequestsSlow and etcdGRPCWriteRequestsSlow next to etcdGRPCRequestsSlow; the machine-config alert is MCCDrainError).
  • Known-issue matching uses signals: the query adds the terms of the alerts, event and container reasons the text carries or implies ("leader changed" for etcd leader elections), and words meet their light stems ("draining" meets "drain"). A match still needs rare words from the text itself. New frozen eval data/evals/known_issue_recall.yaml (51 symptoms written as prose, firing alerts and event lines; 33 everyday errors that must never match) gates recall at 0.8 and false matches at zero: recall went from 36/50 (one false match) to 48/51 (none). kube-noob known-issues 'etcd leader changed frequently, apiserver timeouts after upgrade' --version 4.21.0 now finds OCPBUGS-73881 (4.20.5 finds OCPBUGS-73876; 4.21.2 and 4.20.13, which carry the fixes, find nothing).
  • Signals everywhere: diagnose, debug and bundle list the signals they read (alert names, event, condition and container reasons, admission rules, components) and rank playbooks with them; policy explain lists the admission signals. diagnose -f alert.json takes an Alertmanager webhook payload (or the API's alert list) as it is: each alert is read as text, known issues are matched on each alert's line and summary, and an openshift_version or version label that names an OpenShift 4 release picks the issues for that version. New read-only MCP tool kubenoob_extract_signals; kube-noob signals also prints the alerts a text implies and the playbooks they point at.
  • New playbook DBG-ETCD (39 in all): leader changes, slow disk (WAL fsync and backend commit latency), no leader, members down and the database space alarm, with read-only checks for OpenShift and kubeadm clusters, from the etcd FAQ and maintenance guide and the OpenShift etcd procedures. Every etcd alert in the catalogue points at it.
  • Fixes-as-code memory, entry format: one reviewed YAML file per lesson (entries/MEM-<n>.yaml) with signals, symptom, platform, introduced and fixed versions, operator ranges, cause, fix (text, optional patch, commands), evidence references, outcomes per cluster, owners, sources, dates and an optional risk acceptance. A Pydantic model and a JSON Schema (kube_noob/data/memory/entry.schema.json) ship in the package. Prose is a summary (credentials, raw logs, event rows, stack traces, pipelines and manifests are rejected), a command carries the label the playbook classifier gives it and never prints a Secret, a patch holds manifests but never a Secret, evidence is a URL, ticket key, citation or bundle path, and versions must be quoted.
  • kube-noob memory: init scaffolds a team memory repository (README, schema, entries/, a GitHub Actions workflow and a shell script that run validate); add writes a proposed entry with the next id and never commits; validate is the poisoning guard (schema, duplicates, review dates, credentials anywhere in a file, and the verifier: patches are validated, audited and red-teamed, and privileged SCC, cluster-admin, hostPath and wildcard RBAC grants fail unless the entry records an unexpired riskAccepted); match takes text or an Alertmanager payload, matches approved entries on signals first and distinctive words second, shows negative entries as "tried, did not work" and entries fixed in your version as retired, with "matched N times, resolved M; seen on N of your clusters, fixed on M"; outcome (with a cluster label), stats --by cluster|signal|component and migrate-learning. New demo: kube-noob demo memory.
  • Faster on long input: search walks each distinct query word's postings instead of every chunk for every word (scores unchanged), so a 100 KB repetitive error no longer costs seconds in known-issue matching; JSON input is parsed as JSON only, and each playbook pattern scans the text once.
  • Memory in every runtime: memory.paths in the config (or KUBE_NOOB_MEMORY_PATHS) is read by the CLI, the stdio MCP server and the HTTP server; kube-noob doctor lists each repository with its entry counts and warns about one it cannot read; memory match uses the paths when --dir is not given. Chart: memory.source configMap, pvc or git mounts the repository read-only at /srv/kube-noob/memory; the git source clones it in an init container (https:// or ssh:// with a deploy-key Secret, never credentials in the URL), opens egress only to memory.git.egressCIDRs, names the host in the EgressFirewall, and under config.offline accepts only an in-cluster host.
  • Memory in answers: with memory.paths set, diagnose, known-issues, debug and bundle carry your team's approved entries for the same error as [mem:MEM-<n>] (fix, runbook, tried and did not work, or retired for your version, with outcome counts); the bundle gets a "Team memory" section. New MCP tools kubenoob_memory_match (read-only) and kubenoob_memory_propose (returns the entry's YAML; a local stdio server saves it as proposed only after the user confirmed; the HTTP server never writes). Repository reads are cached until an entry file changes.
  • kube-noob memory import <dir> --from servicenow-json|servicenow-csv|jira-json|jira-csv -f export: resolved tickets become proposed entries with signals from their text and an outcome (resolved, or partial for a workaround) dated when they were resolved; tickets that are open, canceled, closed without a fix or without resolution notes are listed with the reason, and a rerun skips what is already there. People never reach an entry: assignee, caller, reporter and similar fields are not read, and emails, account ids, @mentions, phone numbers, "Assigned to:" lines and "reported by <name>" are removed; a sentence that carries a credential is dropped whole. --dry-run writes nothing. Fixed: an entry with no versions was written with an empty versions: {} block.
  • kube-noob memory replay --memory <dir> --tickets <export> --from <format> [--labels labels.yaml] [--as-of-ticket-date] [--out <dir>]: would your team's memory have helped? Tickets are replayed in the order they were opened and matched on their title and description only; a ticket never matches the entry it became, and with --as-of-ticket-date only entries created before it count (imported entries are dated when their ticket was resolved). The Markdown and JSON report gives the hit rate, hits per signal kind, the misses with their signals and, with labels, the useful-match rate against the 30% gate.
  • The Red Hat Jira copy as a main copy nobody waits for. Each sync now records every ticket whose status, resolution or fix versions changed (fixed, not a bug, won't fix, duplicate, reopened, given a fix version, opened) in jira/changes.jsonl (owner-only, capped at 200,000 events), and a new copy fetches each project's open tickets first. kube-noob corpus jira-changes [--since 2d|36h|2026-10-25] [--project] [--kind] reconciles what changed and lists the open tickets per project and verdict, offline and under the offline lock; the MCP tool kubenoob_ticket_changes returns the same, read-only. kube-noob corpus jira-snapshot -o file.tgz packs tickets, sync state and change log into one owner-only file; corpus import checks its checksums and that it comes from the same Jira before copying anything, and the importing install resumes with the "updated since" pass only. kube-noob serve imports the newest <dataDir>/seed/*.tgz once when it has no copy; chart jira.seed.source image (a private, pinned seed image copied by an init container) or pvc. corpus-refresh keeps the main copy daily (repository variable JIRA_EVERY_DAYS=2 for every two days), continues the previous run's encrypted copy, writes the changes and open tickets to the run summary, and uploads the new copy only encrypted (jira-main-copy-encrypted); Git docs and release history stay weekly.
  • kube-noob corpus jira-audit [--project KEY]: what the local Jira copy holds (tickets per project, verdict and status category, comments kept against the totals Jira reported and the tickets missing some, tickets cut or in the older format, oldest and newest update, key range and coverage, size, sync state), what the anonymiser replaced, and a self-check for emails, account ids and profile links left in the text. Offline. The daily corpus-refresh writes it to the run summary and fails if the self-check finds anything. Syncs report how many requests went to comments.
  • Every enrichment is jittered: the in-cluster Jira sync makes its first run after a random 30 s to 10 min and then runs every jira.sync.intervalHours +/- 10%; clones from GitHub (corpus build, corpus known-issues) are spaced by a random 1 to 4 s; the scheduled corpus-refresh starts at a random offset of up to 15 minutes (the Jira client already spaced requests by the crawl delay plus random jitter).
  • kube-noob corpus advisories (requirements R5.5): Red Hat CSAF VEX statements, licensed CC BY 4.0 (checked on access.redhat.com/security/data), for OpenShift, RHACS, ACM, the layered products and RHEL 8 and 9. Per CVE: severity, CVSS base score and, per product, each component's status (fixed with its erratum, affected with the remediation category, not affected with the VEX justification, under investigation), plus a summary search reads. --cves, --since (from changes.csv) or --from-archive (a downloaded archive, read offline; .tar.zst through zstd). GET-only, HTTPS to Red Hat's security data hosts only (security.access.redhat.com, access.redhat.com), one request a second plus jitter, Retry-After honoured, a request budget, 25 MB per document, 4,000 statements per CVE. The source's licence is checked against the public bundle's allow-list before anything is written: a source not on it stops the build. The redhat-vex shard ships in kube-noob-db with Red Hat's attribution; the db-image workflow refreshes it daily from Red Hat's VEX archive, and CI fetches two CVEs live to prove the format.
  • Private kube-noob-db-jira seed (requirements R1.4, R1.5): kube-noob corpus seal / unseal seal a Jira snapshot with your organisation's key (AES-256-GCM in 1 MiB chunks, a per-file key from PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with 600,000 iterations, chunk counters and a last-chunk flag as nonces, so a wrong key, an edited, reordered, dropped or truncated chunk all fail and nothing is written). corpus import --key-file and the pod's seed import open a *.tgz.enc with the key file named by KUBE_NOOB_SEED_KEY_FILE; a missing or wrong key imports nothing and says "the key does not open ..." while the server keeps serving. Chart jira.seed.keySecret mounts the key from a Secret read-only for the server alone (never in values or the image; fsGroup set off-OpenShift so UID 1001 can read it); jira.seed.path may end in .tgz.enc. Containerfile.seed and a jira-db-image workflow that, after each corpus-refresh, opens the main copy, refuses it if the anonymiser's self-check finds a person, seals it with JIRA_SEED_KEY and pushes it to JIRA_DB_IMAGE, refusing a repository anyone can list before the push and an image anyone can pull after it. serve now states the sync schedule ("jira sync: first run in N s, then every 48 h +/- 10%") or that sync is off. Proven in kind (one leg): a sealed seed answers OCPBUGS-900 under the offline lock, a wrong key fails closed, sync on with 48 h states its jittered schedule.
  • Fixed: corpus import and the seed import extracted the archive into /tmp, which the chart caps at 256 MiB, so a Jira snapshot of a few GB could not seed a pod; archives are now extracted next to the data.
  • The public kube-noob-db image (requirements R1.3): kube-noob corpus db-bundle packs what may be shared (the documentation of every redistributable source with its license, release history, the release-note known issues) into one reproducible kube-noob-db.tgz with ATTRIBUTION.md; shards not marked redistributable and every Jira shard (ticket text and the per-project counts alike) are left out by design. kube-noob corpus db-check is the gate (exit 1): every record redistributable under a license on the allow-list (Apache-2.0, CC-BY-4.0, MIT, BSD, facts from public Git metadata), nothing from Jira, every file listed with its checksum and nothing else in the archive, no links or special files, size and count caps. corpus import recognises the bundle and validates all of it before copying anything. New workflow db-image rebuilds it every day at a random offset from public sources only, gates the archive and the image's own copy (UBI minimal, user 1001, Containerfile.db), and uploads the archive; it pushes to ghcr.io, attests and keyless-signs only when the maintainer sets DB_IMAGE_PUBLISH. Chart db.enabled with db.image.digest (digest only; the chart refuses a tag): an init container copies the archive and serve imports it when it was built after the one the pod already has, so a newer digest refreshes every pod.
  • kube-noob insight (MCP: kubenoob_insight, listed first for anything pasted): names what the input is, with the evidence (Alertmanager payload, events, pod status, logs, ticket, manifests, Helm template, policy denial, build log, Containerfile, SBOM, scanner report, packet capture or .pcap file, must-gather paths, question; several at once), then runs every analyser that applies, each on its own size and time budget (20 s; a late one is named with the command to run it alone): diagnosis with signals, reported issues, team memory and the denial explainer; Red Hat ticket keys in the text (local Jira copy, then the release notes); the audit with its removed-API and schema checks (most severe first) on the manifests, including YAML found inside a mixed paste of events, a denial and a manifest; and the documentation for questions. One deterministic answer: findings each with their source, the signals, read-only next steps, and what was not analysed and why (Helm templates go through helm template first; NetworkPolicy reachability needs policy reach with a source, destination and port; build, Containerfile, SBOM, scanner, capture and must-gather analysers are queued and named). Input with no evidence gets the read-only commands that collect it (oc on OpenShift and ROSA, kubectl elsewhere). Rules only. Recogniser gate on 360 generated cases per seed (every kind plus mixed pastes): precision and recall 1.0 on five seeds; the cases are synthetic and written with the rules, so they guard regressions and the red team's held-out set measures it. Hostile input is bounded: deeply nested JSON and YAML, alias bombs and binary files are recognised as nothing.
  • Fixed: a Pod Security denial quoted without the details in brackets ("violates PodSecurity "restricted:latest": allowPrivilegeEscalation != false") was explained as breaking 0 checks with no patch; the checks are now read from the list itself, counted and patched.
  • Fixed: kubelet's image-pull messages ("Unable to retrieve some image pull secrets ...; attempting to pull the image may not succeed", "Failed to pull image", "Pulling image") matched OCPBUGS-64840 on their fixed wording. Known-issue matching now strips the fixed wording of known component messages and keeps their variable parts (secret and image names). The frozen recall eval gains negative N33 for it (the eval grows; the matcher is tuned, never the eval).
  • kube-noob selftest --cases <file>: your own problems as a test.
